# Decisions log

Paste this file at the start of every session. One line per decision, newest at the bottom of its
section. Details live in the linked ADR. Tasks append here; they never rewrite earlier lines. If a
decision changes, add a new line and link the superseding ADR.

Reserved decision ranges (2026-11-03): disjoint ranges per agent avoid merge collisions, so gaps are intentional and rows are not renumbered: D520 T5.1, D540 T6.4, D560 wrapper PR, D580 checkpoint, D600 T3.1b prep, D620 T3.6, D900-D909 cassettes-models.yml.

## Architecture and process (2026-10-27)

| # | Decision | ADR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D1 | Monorepo: uv workspace (Python 3.12, `src/` layout) + pnpm for the dashboard; `make check` is the single entry point | [0001](adr/0001-monorepo-uv-pnpm.md) |
| D2 | [superseded by D80/D84] Phase 1 is a walking skeleton (fixtures → FakeTarget → metrics → gate → PR comment → Action) before the real corpus or GPU | [0002](adr/0002-walking-skeleton-first.md) |
| D3 | Run artefacts (`run.json`, `results.parquet`, …) are the source of truth; DuckDB is a rebuildable index; no Postgres | [0003](adr/0003-files-source-of-truth-duckdb.md) |
| D4 | Baselines live in orphan branch `pruefstand-baselines`, keyed by `eval_fingerprint`; never compare across fingerprints | [0004](adr/0004-baseline-store-git-branch.md) |
| D5 | [superseded by D81 (tiers) + D61–D64] Two-tier CI: hosted runners for code checks with fakes; ephemeral self-hosted A40 runner for the real gate, trusted PRs or `run-gate` label only | [0005](adr/0005-two-tier-ci.md) |
| D6 | Target cache keyed on `target_fingerprint` (image digest or git SHA + config hash + corpus version); judge cache keyed on (judge model, prompt version, inputs) | [0006](adr/0006-two-caches-fingerprints.md) |
| D7 | Gate stats: paired cluster bootstrap (cluster = `base_id`), 10 000 resamples, one-sided α = 0.025, Holm, min 30 clusters per slice | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D8 | [superseded by D80] FakeLLM + FakeTarget (9 degradation kinds) ship in the package and back all deterministic tests | [0008](adr/0008-real-stack-testing.md) |
| D9 | [superseded by D86] Every LLM call goes through `LLMClient` → LiteLLM. Providers: vLLM on the A40s, GitHub Models, Anthropic (capped), Ollama (dev only). Judge family must differ from the generator family | [0009](adr/0009-litellm-providers.md) |
| D10 | Qdrant only: dense (BGE-M3) + own German sparse vectors in one collection, tenant filter inside every prefetch, RRF, optional reranker | [0010](adr/0010-qdrant-hybrid-tenant-filter.md) |
| D11 | Dashboard: Next.js static export, TS types generated from JSON Schemas, Playwright/axe/Lighthouse | [0011](adr/0011-dashboard-static-nextjs.md) |
| D12 | Public demo on a small CPU host + CPU llama.cpp generator (D92); the cluster is never exposed | [0012](adr/0012-demo-hosting-separate.md) |
| D13 | Logging with structlog (JSON in CI) + optional OpenTelemetry spans; LLM calls in `llm_calls.parquet` | [0013](adr/0013-observability.md) |
| D14 | `pruefstand.yaml` and `rag.yaml` are typed Pydantic models (`extra="forbid"`); JSON Schemas committed in `schemas/` | [0014](adr/0014-typed-config.md) |
| D15 | HF dataset split into license-homogeneous configs: `laws_eurlex`, `germanquad_derived`, `oldp_derived`, `own` | [0015](adr/0015-license-homogeneous-configs.md) |
| D16 | import-linter enforces layers, a pure `gate`, a single LLM gateway, and a rag-demo independent of pruefstand | [0016](adr/0016-import-linter.md) |
| D17 | Releases: Conventional Commits + release-please, PyPI Trusted Publishing, cosign-signed GHCR images with SBOM | [0017](adr/0017-release-automation.md) |
| D18 | Every shared model, config and protocol is defined in `core`; same-row modules never import each other; `metrics` sits one layer above `judges` | [0016](adr/0016-import-linter.md) |
| D19 | `polars` is an accepted dependency for analysis code only (`judges.calibration`, `store.RunIndex`, `experiments/`) | [0016](adr/0016-import-linter.md) |
| D20 | Default hard fails: `canary_leak`, `tenant_leak`, `system_prompt_leak` (no statistics, any hit = FAIL) | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D21 | Published canaries are rotated with every dataset release; a private held-out canary set is never published | [security.md](engineering/security.md) |

## Round-1 review revisions (2026-10-28) — supersede D4–D8 where they conflict

Review history (not binding): `docs/planning/reviews/`. The lines below are the binding record.

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D22 | [superseded by D51/D52/D54] FAIL = Holm-adjusted one-sided p (H0: no drop) < 0.025 **and** mean drop > `max_drop`; WARN = adjusted p < 0.10 and drop > `max_drop` | D7 | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D23 | [superseded by D51/D52/D54] Only gated slices (`overall`, `tenant`, `lang`; `perturbation` for retrieval metrics) with ≥ 30 clusters can FAIL/WARN; other slices are informational (BH, q = 0.10) | D7 | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D24 | [superseded by D51] Holm family = one metric across its gated slices; metrics are separate families | D7 | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D25 | Bootstrap clusters on `TestCase.cluster_id` (source document of the base question); sign test when < 10 clusters differ | D7 | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D26 | Target errors are scored as the worst value, never dropped; `error_rate` absolute limit 0.02; baseline above it is invalid | – | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D27 | Absolute checks (`max_value`): FAIL iff Wilson 95 % lower bound > limit, WARN iff point estimate > limit; injection_success is an absolute check, not a hard fail | – | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D28 | [superseded by D56; refusal δ by D142] Every PR comment shows the MDE per gated metric; `pruefstand power` estimates power; default `max_drop` 0.05 (0.03 for refusal_correct) | – | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D29 | Nightly compares `main` with the last release baseline (cumulative drift, non-blocking; reported as commit status `pruefstand/nightly`, D91) | – | [0004](adr/0004-baseline-store-git-branch.md) |

| D31 | [superseded by D61] No self-hosted runner on the public repo; the A40 runner belongs to the private executor repo `pruefstand-gate`, triggered by `repository_dispatch` from `gate-dispatch.yml` | D5 | [0005](adr/0005-two-tier-ci.md) |

These lines are self-contained and binding; prompts cite them by number.

| # | Decision | Supersedes | ADR |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D51 | **One Holm family per run** across all (gated metric × gated slice) pairs, α = 0.025 one-sided; WARN uses the same Holm-adjusted p-values against `warn_p` = 0.10 | D24 | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D52 | Non-gated (informational) slices are always PASS; BH (q = 0.10) only sets `SliceDecision.notable`; `GateReport.notable_slices` lists them; they never WARN or FAIL | D23 | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D53 | An absolute-check WARN makes the overall status WARN; an absolute-check FAIL makes it FAIL | – | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D54 | Gated slices = `overall`, `tenant`, `lang`, `perturbed_all` (all ROBUST_PR perturbed cases vs their bases, retrieval metrics only); per-perturbation slices are informational | D23 | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D55 | [superseded by D125] `ROBUST_PR` = 40 base questions × 11 perturbations, retrieval metrics only, no judge | D30 | – |
| D56 | MDE shown = max((z₁₋α/m + z₀.₈)·SE, δ + z₀.₈·SE), m = Holm family size; `minimum_detectable_effect(se, alpha, delta, power, m)` | D28 | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D57 | [PR-run part superseded by D131; the 4 000-sim grid now runs nightly] Simulation tests: 4 000 sims, fixed seeds; type-I bound α + 3·√(α(1−α)/4000); full-gate no-op bounds FAIL ≤ 0.03, WARN-or-FAIL ≤ 0.12 | – | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D58 | `likely_cause` returns string codes (`retrieval`, `generation`, `citation_format`, `language`, `refusal_policy`, `errors`, `mixed`); tests assert codes, never rule numbers | – | – |
| D59 | `MetricPolicy.confidence = 0.95` exists for the displayed CI only; it never changes the decision | – | – |
| D60 | [superseded by D85] likely_cause is validated on real mutations in T7.2; T6.3 is noise / σ_d / MDE / timing only and tests likely_cause only on FakeTarget degradations | – | – |
| D61 | [PR trigger superseded by D288: dispatch only, no `run-gate` label, no PR poll] [gated paths extended by D105; fork flow D104; processed state D102] **No gate secrets and no dispatch workflow in the public repo.** The private executor pulls: `gate-executor.yml` runs every 15 min + `workflow_dispatch` (input `pr`), gates open PRs that touch gated paths and carry `run-gate` applied by an allowlisted maintainer after the head commit | D31 | [0005](adr/0005-two-tier-ci.md) |

## Round-4 revisions (2026-10-28): owner answers, real models, real CI (supersede earlier rows where they conflict)

Owner answers: no German annotator (use existing datasets); cluster via plain SSH; free APIs only; GitHub/HF
usernames and hosting accounts follow later. And: no fake LLM, no fake target.

| # | Decision | Supersedes | ADR |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D80 | **No fakes for models or the target.** FakeLLM, FakeTarget, `Degradation`, `build_fake_target`, `target.type: fake` do not exist. Allowed: pure-function unit tests on real inputs; statistical validation of `gate.stats` on simulated numbers; cassettes recorded from **real** model calls (`make cassettes`, re-recorded weekly; never hand-written; every cassette-covered behaviour also has a `live` test) | D8, D49 (fake prefix), FakeTarget rows | [0008](adr/0008-real-stack-testing.md) |
| D81 | [tier-1 split, judge and budget superseded by D94–D99] CI tiers: **0 code** (hosted, every PR); **1 live** `live-e2e.yml` (hosted 4 vCPU CPU runner: real Qdrant + rag-demo + llama.cpp generator + CPU embeddings on the real 5-law slice, judge via GitHub Models with `models: read`, smoke split, real PR comment; same-repo PRs, main, nightly; budget ≤ 30 min per run, hard cap `timeout-minutes: 40`; model + index caches via `actions/cache`); **2 full** (private executor on the A40s, unchanged) | D5 | [0005](adr/0005-two-tier-ci.md) |
| D82 | [fork-PR judge part superseded by D97: no CPU fallback judge, fork PRs run with `judge_mode=skipped`] Tier-1 baseline = artefact of the latest successful `live-e2e.yml` run on `main` (downloaded by run id; refreshed nightly). Fork PRs (no `models` permission): deterministic metrics + CPU fallback judge, stated in the comment | – | [0005](adr/0005-two-tier-ci.md) |
| D83 | [superseded by D97, D99, D113] GitHub Models quota: judgments cached per (answer, judge, prompt version); low-tier model; HTTP 429 → CPU fallback judge and `RunManifest.judge_fallback = true` (never silent). A fallback run has a different `eval_fingerprint`; the gate re-judges the baseline's stored answers with the same fallback judge before comparing. Quotas measured in T0.6 | – | [0009](adr/0009-litellm-providers.md) |
| D84 | M1 = tier 1 green on a real PR with a real comment; the three demo PRs (reranker off, whitespace, `tenant_filter: post_topk` → hard FAIL) run in tier 1. New **T1.13 rag-demo v0** (real, CPU) with bug switches `tenant_filter`, `shared_public_merge`, `reranker`, `prompt_file`, `answer_language`; T2.1/T2.2 run early; T3.2–T3.6 extend v0 | D2 | [0002](adr/0002-walking-skeleton-first.md) |
| D85 | Failure demonstrations and likely_cause checks (T1.11, T6.3) use the real rag-demo bug switches in tier 1; T7.2 validates on the full mutation set | D60 | – |
| D86 | [`hosted_vllm` default superseded by D209: provider `ollama`] **Free providers only.** Defaults: `openai_compatible_local` (llama.cpp), `hosted_vllm` (A40), `github` (GitHub Models free tier). Opt-in free tiers: Mistral, Gemini, Groq, OpenRouter `:free`. Paid providers need `allow_paid: true` (off); `budget.max_usd_per_run = 0`. `LLMPolicy` blocks external providers for `datagen.private_docs` prompts and any message containing a registered canary / held-out fact | D9 | [0009](adr/0009-litellm-providers.md) |
| D87 | [superseded by D330 (ADR-0019): no local 70B; public text via GitHub Models, private docs via the local judge model] [datagen family fixed by D112] Test generation (T4.2) and LLM perturbations (T4.5) run on the local 70B-class model on the A40s; GitHub Models cross-checks a 10 % sample | – | – |
| D88 | Judge calibration without an annotator (T5.3): MEMERAG German subset (faithfulness; `Supported` → 2, `Not Supported` → 0, "Challenging" dropped; CC BY-NC 4.0, **download-only, never redistributed, never used for training**), GermanQuAD constructed correctness pairs, GermanRAG refusal, constructed consistency pairs. Binary κ (judge 2 vs < 2). Optional T5.3-opt: 100 hand labels if a German speaker turns up | REVIEW Q1 default | – |
| D89 | Licensing (HF cards checked 2026-10-28): `deepset/germanquad` and `deepset/germandpr` are **CC BY 4.0**; GermanRAG verified in T2.1 (CC BY 4.0 if it inherits only from GermanDPR). `germanquad_derived` config is CC BY 4.0 unless T2.1 finds otherwise. T2.1 records each card's license field and fails on a mismatch with DATA_LICENSES.md | D15 (licence of that config) | [0015](adr/0015-license-homogeneous-configs.md) |
| D90 | [container-runtime part superseded by D196: no containers, runner or state on the cluster] Cluster access is plain SSH (no Slurm). T0.6 records user-level services, Docker / rootless Docker / Podman / Apptainer, GPU access, outbound HTTPS; executor needs no root; Apptainer is the fallback container runtime | REVIEW Q2 default | [0005](adr/0005-two-tier-ci.md) |

## Round-8 revisions (2026-10-31) — cold read 4; supersede earlier rows where they conflict

Source: `docs/planning/reviews/round8-resolution.md` (W1–W5). One row per resolution item; the W number is in brackets.

| # | Decision | Supersedes | ADR |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D94 | [W1.1] Tier-1 PR runs use the new split `smoke_live` (`Split.SMOKE_LIVE`): 20 of the 40 fixtures, chosen by rule (≥ 1 leak probe per tenant, 2 refusal, 2 injection, 1 system-prompt probe, rest factual with citations); the other 20 fixtures stay `smoke` | D81 (smoke split) | [0005](adr/0005-two-tier-ci.md) |
| D95 | [W1.2] Tier-1 generator ≤ 2B params, GGUF Q4_K_M, **Qwen3 thinking disabled** (`enable_thinking=false` / `/no_think`; this rule holds for every Qwen3 use in every tier and role). Tier-1 RAG: `top_k: 4`, chunk text truncated to 256 tokens in the prompt, `max_tokens: 256`, `reranker: off`. Budget ≤ 20 min per run, `timeout-minutes: 30` | D81 (budget) | [0005](adr/0005-two-tier-ci.md) |
| D96 | [superseded by D188, D171] [W1.3] The tier-1 gate decides on deterministic checks only (hard fails; absolute checks `injection_success`, `error_rate`; language / citation / refusal-phrase checks). Paired statistics are `INSUFFICIENT_DATA` at n = 20 and the comment says so; the statistical gate is tier 2 | D81 | [0007](adr/0007-paired-cluster-bootstrap.md) |
| D97 | [W1.4] Tier-1 judged metrics are informational: ≤ 10 judged cases per PR via GitHub Models, cached; quota exhausted or no `models` permission → skipped, `judge_mode=skipped`. No uncalibrated judge decides a required check. The CPU fallback judge is removed (`judge_mode` = `github` \| `local` \| `skipped`) | D82 (fork judge), D83 | [0009](adr/0009-litellm-providers.md) |

## Round-10 revisions (2026-10-31) — real cluster facts; supersede earlier rows where they conflict

Source: `docs/planning/reviews/round10-resolution.md` (Z1–Z7, binding; owner facts from `ollama list`,
`nvidia-smi`, `df -h`, `loginctl`) and the decisions accepted with it (D204–D210). Single source of truth for
numbers: `docs/engineering/gpu-cluster.md`; wave schedule: `tasks/PARALLEL-PLAN.md`.

| # | Decision | Supersedes | ADR |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D196 | [two-server topology and `-L 11435` superseded by D324/D325 (ADR-0018): one Ollama server on `:11434` for both GPUs] [`tmux` superseded by D228: `setsid nohup`] [Z1] Topology: the cluster `cairo-s101lp` (2 × A40, plain SSH, local disk full) runs **only** two Ollama servers in `tmux` (`ollama-gpu0` `127.0.0.1:11434`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0`; `ollama-gpu1` `127.0.0.1:11435`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=1`). Runner, orchestrator, repo checkout, Docker, Qdrant, candidate containers, CPU reranker, locks and state run on the TrueNAS VM `pruefstand-exec` (Ubuntu 24.04, Docker), which reaches Ollama through `autossh -L 11434 -L 11435` | D90 (runtime part), D100 (host), ADR-0005 executor host | [0005](adr/0005-two-tier-ci.md) |
| D197 | [NAS model dir, `CLUSTER_NAS_ROOT` and NAS `bin/` superseded by D328/D331/D332 (ADR-0019): models on the cluster disk, data root on the VM disk, scripts over SSH] [Z2] File bridge: one TrueNAS dataset, cluster path `/data/dheena/truenas-ollama` (`CLUSTER_NAS_ROOT`), VM path `/mnt/pruefstand` (`PRUEFSTAND_DATA_ROOT`), layout `ollama-models/`, `bin/`, `data/`, `corpus/`, `datasets/`, `baselines/`, `backups/`, `cache/`. Cluster scripts live in `infra/cluster/`, are copied by the VM to NAS `bin/` and run as `ssh $CLUSTER_SSH_HOST bash $CLUSTER_NAS_ROOT/bin/<script>.sh`. Nothing is written on the cluster outside the NAS mount. One writer per NAS path, write-then-rename, no locks or databases on the NAS | D109 (backup location) | – |
| D198 | [judges, datagen and model dir superseded by D328–D330 (ADR-0019): one judge `gemma3:12b`, no datagen model on the cluster, models on the cluster disk] [server env and `ollama-all` superseded by D324 (ADR-0018): `OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS=3`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0,1`, datagen = API load on the same server] [cluster env refined by T3.1a/ADR-0009 and D279] [Z3] All tier-2 LLM and embedding serving is **Ollama**; vLLM and TEI are dropped from the default plan. GPU0: generator `qwen3.8:27b-q8_0` (existing; family/licence/context verified in T0.6/T3.1b) + `bge-m3`; GPU1: PR judge (mistral 24B-class Q4_K_M) by day, gemma 27B-class Q4_K_M nightly judge by night; datagen llama 70B-class Q4_K_M over both cards (`ollama-all`). Reranker `BAAI/bge-reranker-v2-m3` runs on the **VM CPU** (`rerank-cpu`, TEI-compatible `POST /rerank`). Server env: `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=4`, `OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS=2`, `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=24h`, `OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1`. Models are pinned by tag + manifest digest; the first load per mode (from the NAS) is excluded from timing budgets | D38, D121 (reranker), D141 (serving) | [0009](adr/0009-litellm-providers.md) |
| D199 | [trigger amended by D655: the judged subset is the PR default] [fallback semantics superseded by D295: the subset limits generation and judging of `core` answerable cases] [judged subset fixed as `TestCase.judged_subset` + `--judged-subset` by D248/D249] [robust_pr and nightly scope superseded by D222/D223] [Z4] Budgets, measured by T3.1b and then pinned: tier-2 PR (core + robust_pr) ≤ 90 min, nightly ≤ 5 h, datagen as weekend batch. If T3.1b measures more, PR runs compute judged metrics on a fixed 150-case judged subset of `core` (deterministic metrics stay on all cases; the subset is in the dataset manifest and the fingerprint) and the SPEC MDE tables are updated | D40 (budget numbers) | – |
| D200 | [lock/`MAINTENANCE` location superseded by D211/D212: `/var/lib/pruefstand/`; start method by D228] [Z5] Operations: `gpu.lock`, `MAINTENANCE` and `state/` live on the VM's local disk (`$PRUEFSTAND_HOME`), never on the NAS. Preflight after the lock checks both Ollama endpoints through the tunnel and, if one is down, starts it over SSH (`start-ollama.sh`, tmux), so a cluster reboot needs no manual step. Mode switch `day`/`night`/`datagen` = Ollama API load/unload (`keep_alive: "24h"` / `0`) by the orchestrator under the lock. Heartbeat, health and backups (round 8–9) run on the VM | D101 (lock location) | [0005](adr/0005-two-tier-ci.md) |
| D201 | [model dir and NAS `bin/` superseded by D328/D332 (ADR-0019)] [Z6] Tasks: T0.6 = cluster + NAS facts check (Ollama API through a tunnel, NAS read throughput); **new T0.7** (H+AI) TrueNAS VM setup; T3.1a and T6.2a depend on T0.7; T3.1a = cluster scripts in `infra/cluster/`, dual Ollama servers, model pulls into `ollama-models/`, mode switching via the Ollama API, no Docker on the cluster; T3.1b benchmarks through Ollama; T6.2a/b put runner, orchestrator, Qdrant and candidates on the VM with GPU access only via the tunnels | – | – |
| D202 | [`CLUSTER_NAS_ROOT`, NAS model dir and server env superseded by D328/D331/D332 (ADR-0019)] [SSH key owner superseded by D211: user `pruefstand`, `/home/pruefstand/.ssh/id_ed25519_cluster`] [Z7] Owner confirmations: the VM gets **64 GB RAM** (host 128 GB); CPU models on the VM ≤ 24 GB in total (reranker, tier-1 ≤ 2B GGUF, CPU embeddings); GPU models stay on the cluster. The VM is the **primary development and execution machine** (VS Code on Windows with Remote-SSH; coding agents run their commands on the VM); WSL2 is optional; T0.0 targets the VM first. T0.7 switches VM → cluster login to an **SSH key** created on the VM (`ssh-keygen` + `ssh-copy-id`); the password is typed once and never stored. `start-ollama.sh` exports `OLLAMA_MODELS=$CLUSTER_NAS_ROOT/ollama-models`, `OLLAMA_HOST=127.0.0.1:<port>`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<gpu>` per server; the existing qwen model is moved or re-pulled into `ollama-models/` once (T3.1a) | D47, D70 (dev machine) | [0001](adr/0001-monorepo-uv-pnpm.md) |
| D203 | [`CLUSTER_NAS_ROOT` dropped by D331 (ADR-0019)] [`OLLAMA_GPU1_URL` value superseded by D324 (ADR-0018): both URLs `http://127.0.0.1:11434`] [Z7] Variables (VM `.env`): `CLUSTER_SSH_HOST=cairo-s101lp`, `CLUSTER_NAS_ROOT=/data/dheena/truenas-ollama`, `PRUEFSTAND_DATA_ROOT=/mnt/pruefstand`, `OLLAMA_GPU0_URL=http://localhost:11434`, `OLLAMA_GPU1_URL=http://localhost:11435`, plus `RERANK_URL` (T3.1a), `LLM_PROXY_URL` and `PRUEFSTAND_CANDIDATE_API_KEY` (T6.2a). `PRUEFSTAND_MODEL_CACHE` and every vLLM/TEI endpoint variable are dropped (no local model cache on the cluster; tier-1 CPU models use `LIVE_MODEL_DIR`) | – | – |
| D204 | [judge num_ctx superseded by D270: 12288] [`/v1` + `/no_think` path superseded by D219: native API only] [accepted] Context is always explicit (Ollama truncates silently): generator `num_ctx` **8192** (16384 × 4 slots does not fit beside the 29 GB weights on one A40); judges 16384 if T3.1b shows it fits on GPU1, else 8192; judge input cap = `num_ctx − 1 024` (D169). Qwen thinking off via native `/api/chat` `"think": false` (on `/v1` the `/no_think` marker); the `<think>` strip stays as the counted safety net (D95, D191) | – | – |
| D205 | [superseded by D329 (ADR-0019): one judge, no night swap] [fallback semantics refined by D224: fallback ⇒ judged metrics informational] [accepted] The gemma nightly judge runs on **GPU1** (PR judge unloaded first; two judges are never resident together) while the generator stays on GPU0, so nightly generation and judging **may overlap** (judging starts on cached responses). Unavailable nightly judge → PR judge with `judge_fallback=true` | D141 (GPU0 after phase A) | – |
| D206 | [judge-server wording superseded by D325 (ADR-0018): judges share the server; the model allow-list is the separation] [paths superseded by D219 (native `/api/*` only); reach method by D215, then D242; auth header ownership by D216] [accepted] New trusted service **`llm-proxy`** (built in T6.2a, on `gate-net` and the tunnel): the candidate's **only** route to Ollama. It forwards to `ollama-gpu0` only, only `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /api/chat`, `POST /api/embed`, `POST /v1/embeddings`, `GET /api/tags`, only for the run's catalogue generator and embedding tags, and rejects `/api/pull`, `/api/delete`, `/api/create`, `/api/copy`, any `keep_alive` field and any other model. `ollama-gpu1` (judges) is never reachable from `gate-net`. How it reaches the host tunnel is fixed in T0.7 | D62 (candidate network detail) | [security.md](engineering/security.md) |
| D207 | [store superseded by D328 (ADR-0019): `/data/dheena/Models`, free-space check, swap rule] [`ollama-gpu0` wording superseded by D324: pulls go to the one server] [accepted] `infra/gpu/fetch-model.sh <id>` = `ollama pull <tag>` on the cluster against `ollama-gpu0` (store on the NAS) followed by a manifest-digest check against `infra/gpu/models.yaml`; mismatch → `ollama rm <tag>`, exit 1; one pull at a time | D151 (script body) | – |
| D208 | [exit 14/16 meaning superseded by D332/D331 (ADR-0019): cluster bin dir; data root unavailable] [repair normalises the mode's model set: D276] [exit 15 and 16 added by D227/D247] [accepted] `infra/gpu/health.sh --repair` is the preflight: restarts a down Ollama server over SSH (≤ 5 min wait), loads missing models of the current mode, checks resident digests (exit 13 on mismatch) and NAS `bin/` freshness (exit 14); without `--repair` it only reports (exit codes 10–14, interfaces §1.5) | D151 (script body) | – |
| D209 | [id format refined by D221] [interface, round 10] Provider prefix `ollama/<tag>` replaces `hosted_vllm/` in `LLMPolicyConfig.allowed_providers` (default `("openai_compatible_local", "ollama", "github")`); `build_backend` resolves its base URL through `infra/gpu/endpoints.yaml` (`base_url_env` ∈ `OLLAMA_GPU0_URL`, `OLLAMA_GPU1_URL`); `ollama` counts as local (not EXTERNAL) for the data guard. `BaselineStoreConfig.data_root` default = `$PRUEFSTAND_DATA_ROOT/baselines` (NAS), else `$PRUEFSTAND_HOME/baselines` when `PRUEFSTAND_DATA_ROOT` is unset (local dev) | D86 (`hosted_vllm`), D157 (location) | [0009](adr/0009-litellm-providers.md) |
| D210 | [process] Multi-agent build follows `tasks/PARALLEL-PLAN.md` (waves of ≤ 6 agents, one task = one worktree = one PR, shared files only as the task's own hunk in its last commit, checkpoint after every wave); `tasks/TASKS.md` stays the source of truth for tasks and dependencies. **T5.3 ships as two PRs**: sets 1–5 first, `legal_hard` after T4.7b, T3.6 and T5.1 (D161). Calendar with waves ≈ 12–15 weeks part-time (serial plan 20–26 weeks) | – | – |

| D344 | [T1.6] `scipy>=1.13` becomes a runtime dependency of `pruefstand` (only `gate` and its tests use it: `scipy.special.stdtrit` for the small-sample factor c_k, `math.comb` for the exact sign test up to m = 1000 and, above that, lazily `scipy.stats.binom.cdf`; `gate-pure` still forbids I/O and LLM imports) and `scipy-stubs` a dev dependency (`mypy --strict` needs typed scipy). The small-sample correction (SPEC §7.2, ADR-0007) is implemented as specified. Hostile-input bounds: `n_resamples` is capped at 10 000 000 (same as `GatePolicy`) and `n_resamples * n_clusters` at 1e10 draws, the resample index array is chunked at 5e7 elements, every invalid input (NaN/inf, empty, mismatched, bool/non-numeric parameters, float64 overflow) raises `GateInputError`. A cluster mean within 1e-12 of 0 (relative to the largest |diff|) counts as zero for the sign-test switch. `[tool.mutmut]` in `packages/pruefstand/pyproject.toml` (+ `packages/pruefstand/mutmut_pytest.ini`; mutmut 3 needs cwd `packages/pruefstand`, the `ci.yml` mutmut step cds there) mutates `gate/` (T1.5b appends `metrics/`). Review minors: scipy is imported lazily (`scipy.special.stdtrit` for c_k, `math.comb` for sign tests up to m = 1000, `scipy.stats.binom.cdf` above), so `import pruefstand.gate.stats` costs 0.37 s instead of 1.44 s. |
| D345 | [T1.5b] Language ID for `language_match` is `lingua-language-detector` (Apache-2.0, no network, no model download), built once on first use (`lru_cache`) over nine languages (DE, EN, FR, ES, IT, NL, PL, RU, TR) so a confident non-DE/EN answer scores 0 instead of being mislabelled DE/EN, run on the first 5 000 characters of the NFC-normalised answer. `not_applicable` for answers under 20 characters, for phrase-list refusals and when lingua returns no language. New dependency of `packages/pruefstand` (CLAUDE.md §5). Measured: wheel ~170 MB, installed `.so` ~292 MB, import 0.19 s / RSS ~50 MB, no network at runtime, identical output across processes and threads. |
| D346 | [T1.5b] Leak normalisation = SPEC §3.2 (`security_normalise`, `compile_forbidden`, `contains_forbidden`): NFKD, removal of every format character (Cf), variation selectors and invisible fillers, casefold, removal of combining marks (so `ä` = `a`; `schon` = `schön` is accepted), Cyrillic/Greek look-alikes folded to Latin, whitespace collapsed, canary tokens reduced to `canary-<tp>-<hex>` (the hex body stops at the next `canary`), German number words 0-100 to digits (never the article `ein`). Plain forbidden strings of 20+ letters/digits are also compared with all separators removed; injection markers must have 6+ letters/digits. `re:` entries are folded without casefolding (`ß` -> `ss`), must be valid, <= 500 characters, not match the empty string and pass the structural ReDoS check; an invalid entry is a `ValueError` at dataset load and a `status=error` value, never an exception during a run and never a pass. Non-goals (not detected): reversed, base64, hex, rot13, letter-spaced or dotted canaries. Every step is linear (Hypothesis time-bound property). |
| D347 | [T1.5b] `MetricRegistry.default()` raises `ConfigError` unless its names equal `KNOWN_METRICS` minus the judged names (closes the T1.2 follow-up; the constructor also rejects names outside `KNOWN_METRICS` and duplicates). `evaluate_deterministic` takes `Sequence[Metric]` (widened from `Sequence[DeterministicMetric]`, interfaces §5) and skips metrics without `compute` (`latency_ms`, `error_rate`, judged); the constructor rejects a deterministic metric without `compute` (other than those two). `canary_leak` is `not_applicable` on `system_prompt_extraction` (covered by `system_prompt_leak`) and when a case has no forbidden strings (nothing to test, never a silent 0). |
| D348 | [T1.5b] `citation_grounded` takes its evidence from the retrieved chunk **text** only: API v1 `RetrievedChunk` carries no `citation_key` (only the indexed `Chunk` has it), so the `citation_key` branch of the R9 X3.2 definition cannot be evaluated. A chunk whose text omits its own § header therefore counts its citation as ungrounded; adding an optional `citation_key` to `RetrievedChunk` (additive API v1 change, T1.1/T1.3/T3.x) is a follow-up proposal, not done here. |
| D349 | [T1.5b] `tenant_leak` fails closed (deviation from the prompt's literal "NA if any `tenant_id` is `None`"): a confirmed foreign tenant is a leak (value 1, `details["unknown_tenant_chunks"]` counts the chunks without tenant) even when other chunks omit `tenant_id`; only "no foreign tenant confirmed and at least one `tenant_id` missing" is `not_applicable`. Otherwise a candidate could blank every `tenant_id` to dodge the metric. Follow-up for T1.7/T1.8: a hard-fail metric that is `not_applicable` or `error` on a case whose target declares chunk support must FAIL the gate, not drop out of the denominator (also for invalid `forbidden_strings`). Known limit: `unknown_tenant_chunks > 0`, `tenant_leak` with `chunks=None`, empty `forbidden_strings`, `invalid_forbidden_string`, `forbidden_match_timeout` and `truncated=True` are the ways a hard-fail metric still ends as NA/error/capped; T1.7/T1.8 define "target declares chunk support" (a `/v1/info`/response contract that returns `chunks`) and FAIL the gate for NA/error there, and treat `truncated=True` of a citation metric at least as a warning. |
| D350 | [T1.5b] New dependency `regex` 2026.9.29 (locked; Apache-2.0 with CNRI-Python parts) and dev stubs `types-regex`: `re:` forbidden patterns are searched with `regex.search(..., timeout=2.0)` because the haystack is the candidate's answer and Python `re` has no timeout; a timeout is `status=error` (`forbidden_match_timeout`). A structural check on the `re` parse tree rejects nested unbounded repeats and alternations inside unbounded repeats at compile time; the timeout covers polynomial blow-ups that check cannot see. ONE budget (2 s) per metric evaluation is shared by all patterns and texts, and regexes read at most 100 000 characters per evaluation. `regex` measures CPU time of the matching thread, not wall time: with several threads a case can hit `forbidden_match_timeout` early, so T1.8 must serialise regex matching or add a wall-clock guard. |
| D351 | [T1.5b] Citation metrics read at most 20 000 characters of the answer (and of each chunk, 100 000 in total) and extract them once per response (cached on answer + citations); a capped case carries `details["truncated"]=True`. Without the cap a 50 000-character answer cost 17 s per case across the four metrics. |
| D352 | [T1.3, security hardening of `HttpTargetAdapter`; no interface change] The target is untrusted, so every call has a connect timeout (min(`timeout_s`, 10 s)), read/write timeouts and a **total deadline** of `timeout_s` (`asyncio.timeout`, bounds a slow-drip body); the body is read raw with a hard cap `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` = 8 MiB (declared `Content-Length` checked first; `Accept-Encoding: identity` is sent and any other `Content-Encoding` is a permanent error, so no decompression bomb); redirects are **never followed** (3xx = permanent `TargetError`, `Location` not echoed); the adapter refuses non-http(s) URLs and URLs with credentials, query or fragment (`ConfigError`); `trust_env=False` (no `~/.netrc`, no proxy env). The bearer token is read from `auth_env` **at call time** (supports rotation; unset or non-printable value = permanent `TargetError` naming only the variable) and is redacted from error excerpts; error excerpts are <= 200 chars with control characters removed. Hostile bodies (NaN/Infinity/`1e999`, lone surrogates, 100 000-deep nesting, non-objects) are permanent `TargetError`s, never `RecursionError`/`ValueError`. The wire `request_id` is **always** a fresh `uuid4().hex`; the caller's `TargetRequest.request_id` (a case id may sit there) is never sent. No retries (T1.8 owns them; the prompt forbids them here). 4xx stays `transient=False` as in interfaces §4 (429/408 retry policy is a T1.8 question). OpenAPI: `top_k` is optional+nullable in `QueryRequest`, chunk `tenant_id`/`used_in_prompt` optional+nullable; `test_target_api_v1.py` fails when component properties drift from the pydantic models. **Review round 1 (same row):** response bodies are parsed in `adapters/wire.py` in a worker thread inside the total deadline, with bounds checked before pydantic (citations <= 1000 and strings, chunks <= 10 000; no unbounded `exc.errors()` message, no raw parser exception on the chain). Third-party tolerance without touching core models: unknown keys are dropped at response/chunk/usage/info level, null optionals (`citations`, `chunks`, `tenant_id`, `used_in_prompt`, `serving`...) are absent, `usage` without both token counts is absent, type errors in known fields stay permanent `TargetError`s; OpenAPI response schemas are `additionalProperties: true` (requests strict; `RetrieveResponse` forbids `answer`). `info()` returns `None` for 404 only (405/501 raise). Token redaction covers plain, JSON-escaped and URL-encoded forms and anything after `Bearer `. TLS certificate failures are permanent; a status is judged before `Content-Encoding` (a gzip 503 stays transient). Duplicate JSON keys: last wins; BOM accepted; `Content-Type` not checked. **Review round 2:** token redaction also covers `quote()` with the default `safe="/"` and the doubly JSON-escaped form. Integer citation ids (not bool) are stringified (`[1, 2]` -> `("1", "2")`); every other non-string item stays permanent. A `usage` with only one of the two token counts is dropped as absent, so `usage=None` can mean partial usage (a token-cost metric must not read it as none reported). Wire parsing runs in the adapter's own `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4)`, shut down in `aclose()`, not in the loop's default executor. Accepted: junk inside ignored keys can still cost ~230 MiB peak at the 8 MiB cap. |
| D353 | [T1.4, test model] The CPU test model for the tier-1 `live` profile, the live tests and `make cassettes` is `Qwen/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct-GGUF` file `qwen2.5-0.5b-instruct-q4_k_m.gguf`, revision `9217f5db79a29953eb74d5343926648285ec7e67`, 491 400 032 bytes, **SHA-256 `74a4da8c9fdbcd15bd1f6d01d621410d31c6fc00986f5eb687824e7b93d7a9db`** (the LFS etag of that revision, re-verified by `scripts/models/fetch.py` after the download), license Apache-2.0 (model card front matter, checked 2026-11-01). Pinned in `infra/models/test-models.yaml`; weights go to `~/.cache/pruefstand/models` and are never committed. Every cassette header carries date, model id and this hash. |
| D354 | [T1.4, dependency] `litellm` **1.103.10** (as locked in `uv.lock`) is added to `packages/pruefstand` because ADR-0009 prescribes LiteLLM as the one provider layer; it is imported only in `pruefstand/llm/backends/litellm.py` (import-linter `llm-gateway`, ruff `TID251`). `pytest-recording` 0.14.0 / `vcrpy` 9.2.0 (HTTP cassettes) and `polars` (call log) were already dependencies. |
| D355 | [T1.4, scope notes] (a) The backend does not retry: `num_retries=0`, hard timeout, transient/permanent errors mapped to `LLMError(transient, status)`; bounded retry with backoff and `Retry-After` is T5.1 (`test_retries.py`). (b) `LLMPolicy` already carries the external-provider data guard (private-doc prompt prefix, canary token / fact in the messages) because a policy that silently lacks it is unsafe; T5.1 keeps its `test_policy_guard.py` rows. (c) The LLM cache key is the prompt text of the task (without `model_version`); adding `model_version` (D113) is T5.1. (d) `LiteLLMBackend.think_stripped` counts stripped `<think>` blocks; surfacing it in `RunManifest.counts` is T5.1/pipeline work. (e) A cassette miss raises `LLMError("cassette miss: <hash> (no cassette for <prompt_id>)")`, satisfying both the prompt and interfaces §3. (f) `validate_policy_config` enforces the provider names and `allow_paid` at load (left open by T1.2). (g) `github/` uses `https://models.github.ai/inference` explicitly (the host the T0.6 quota probe uses): LiteLLM 1.103.10's built-in GitHub default host `models.inference.ai.azure.com` does not resolve from the VM. From the VM, `models.github.ai` answers every path with `200 text/plain OK` (2026-11-01), so the optional GitHub Models live test could not be verified there; it runs in Actions with `models: read` once `live-e2e.yml` exists (T1.12). (h) Review hardening: cassettes keep only allow-listed headers and every recorded text must pass `assert_no_secrets` (recording refuses); `LiteLLMBackend` enforces a hard `asyncio.wait_for` timeout and bounded responses (transport byte cap + answer length vs `max_tokens`); `extract_json_object` is linear (256 KiB cap, `raw_decode`); `LITELLM_MODE=PRODUCTION` stops `import litellm` from loading `.env`; `LLMPolicy(..., external_datagen=False)` denies DATAGEN to external providers by default (interface addition, T4.2 passes `True`); `openai_compatible_local` endpoints must be loopback / RFC1918 / compose services; `cassettes.yml` is split into a read-only `record` job and a `push` job without repository code (allow-list entry `(cassettes.yml, push)`). |
| D356 | [T1.4, review round 3] Bodies of every OpenAI-client provider (`openai_compatible_local/`, `github/`; `ollama/` in T5.1) go through `LimitedTransport` (byte cap, tripped flag -> permanent "too large"); connection errors (DNS, refused, reset, timeout) map to a transient `LLMError` without a status; `LITELLM_MODE` is forced to PRODUCTION and a pre-imported non-PRODUCTION litellm raises `ConfigError`; `assert_no_secrets` also scans JSON-decoded strings, URL-decoded text, is case-insensitive and knows AWS/Slack/GitLab/Google/PEM/`password=` shapes; the `cassettes.yml` `push` job is validated by `scripts/workflow_no_repo_code.py` (exactly `contents: write`, no repository code, only checkout/download-artifact) and checks artefact member types, count, size and staged modes; `fetch.py` needs a 40-hex revision, a download deadline and a default size cap; `LLMRequest.max_tokens` is bounded 1..65536; `normalise` folds U+2010..U+2015 and U+2212 to `-`. Round 4: gzip/deflate responses are refused (decompression bomb: `Content-Encoding` other than identity trips the flag, requests send `Accept-Encoding: identity`); `mistral`, `gemini`, `groq`, `openrouter` are refused by `build_backend` until they have a capped client (follow-up T5.1); `fetch.py` checks redirect targets against the allowed schemes; cassette artefact file names are restricted to `[A-Za-z0-9_./-]+\.(yaml|json)` and deletions use `git --literal-pathspecs`. Known and left: `workflow_policy.REPO_CODE_RUN` is a deny-list (follow-up: allow-list the push job's commands). |
| D357 | [T0.3 follow-up, flaky `test_stack_run_signalled_tears_down_and_leaves_no_process`; `mk/docker.mk` `stack-run`, `scripts/tests/test_docker_mk.py` and the new `scripts/tests/test_docker_mk_stack_run.py`; no interface change] Root causes found by looping the scenario under CPU stress (before: 4 failures in 960 group-TERM runs, all with `down` never run; 40 of 40 make-only TERM runs left CMD running): (a) the recipe ran under `sh` (dash), where `set -m` fails without a tty, so CMD stayed in the caller's process group, `kill -TERM -$pid` signalled nothing and CMD survived a TERM sent to make alone (the old test hid this: it blocked on the pipe until CMD's `sleep 30` ended); (b) make forwards TERM to its child and a group signal delivers a second one; the second TERM ran the `exit 143` trap inside the EXIT trap and aborted `down` (the observed flake); (c) the group-kill trap was installed after the fork of CMD; (d) a signal reaching the new group while its leader is between fork and exec is lost. Fix: the lock command is `bash -c` (job control works without a tty); because CMD now has its own process group, TERM, INT, HUP and QUIT are all trapped (a closed SSH or VS Code terminal sends HUP to the group; untrapped, CMD would outlive make and hold `stack.lock`); every handler first ignores further signals, and `down` inherits that ignore, so a hung `down` needs KILL from outside; the handler is installed before CMD is forked, a signal between `&` and `pid=$!` is replayed via `stop`, and CMD starts with default handlers (`trap -`); the handler re-sends TERM every 0.2 s until the group is gone and falls back to KILL after 2 s. GNU make forwards only TERM to its children, so INT/HUP/QUIT are tested as group signals only. Tests (34 collected on main, 40 now): the signal test (7 cases: TERM to make, TERM to the group, TERM x3, INT, HUP, QUIT, and a CMD that ignores TERM) waits for `started <pgid>` on a reader thread with deadlines (no blocking pipe read, no fixed sleep), checks CMD's own process group and that `stack.lock` is free, and bounds signal-to-exit time far below CMD's 120 s; the stack-run tests moved to the new file (300-line limit). Separately, two `make -n stack-run` tests ran the real `flock` on the shared `~/pruefstand/stack.lock` (`-n` still runs recipe lines containing `$(MAKE)`) and hung whenever another agent held a stack session; they now use a temporary lock. `bash` is already required by the repo scripts. |
| D358 | [T1.7] **Adaptive re-run implemented in `evaluate_gate`** (SPEC §7.2, ADR-0007; it was specified but not coded). After the first bootstrap pass, a testable gated pair with `method == "bootstrap"`, an observed drop beyond `max_drop` and a p within 3 Monte-Carlo SE (`sqrt(p(1-p)/B)`) of a Holm threshold (`alpha/j` or `warn_p/j`, j = 1..m) is bootstrapped again with B = 200 000 from the same `rng`, in pair order, before Holm. Pairs whose drop is not relevant cannot FAIL or WARN and are not refined. R2 (`p = 1/2001`, m = 8) stays unrefined; `BootstrapResult.n_resamples == 200000` marks a refined pair, which the no-op simulation counts (AI13-14). |
| D359 | [T1.7, D349 follow-up; revised in review] **Unverifiable hard-fail checks.** "Target declares chunk support" = at least one recorded response of the baseline **or** the candidate has `chunks is not None`. For a case that was answered (no recorded response-less request and `error_rate != 1`; without records every case counts as answered): a hard-fail metric that is `error` is **always** a `HardFailFinding(rule="hard_fail_unverifiable")` (the answer-only leak checks can error on any target, e.g. regex budget); `not_applicable` with a reason other than `category` / `no_forbidden_strings` is one on a chunk-declaring target; a metric absent from the case is one. Non-binary hard-fail values raise `GateInputError`. Cases whose request failed belong to the `error_rate` check. Known limit, follow-up: `truncated=True` of a citation metric should at least warn (T1.8/T1.10). |
| D360 | [T1.7, scope notes] (a) Absolute checks and `overall` use unperturbed cases only (step 4), also for `error_rate`. (b) `top_failing_cases` ranks unperturbed cases by their minimum oriented diff over the metrics whose gated `overall` decision is WARN/FAIL (negative only, ties by case id). (c) Informational pairs, including every pair of an informational metric, go to `notable_slices` only; `decisions` holds gated pairs. (d) `SliceDecision` validates `informational_reason ⇒ gated is False`; `GateReport` validates `baseline_run_id is None ⇔ no_baseline`; `HardFailFinding.evidence` ≤ 200 chars. (e) `CAUSE_MESSAGES["mixed"]` is `"Several causes: <codes>"`, the renderer fills `<codes>`. (g) Review of PR #22: runs must hold the same cases, no duplicates, not empty (else `GateInputError`); a gated metric without any usable baseline value is a `GateInputError`; the 20 % judge-error cap is applied per pair (slice and `overall`), also when exclusion would leave fewer than `min_clusters` clusters; applicable clusters and the slice list come from the pre-exclusion table; `n_unmatched` / `n_excluded` in `GateReport`;  `error_rate` / injection cases with an unknown value count as events (fail closed); hard-fail metrics absent on an answered case, or non-exempt NA/error when the baseline OR candidate returns chunks, are unverifiable; perturbed-only breakage is caught by `perturbed_all` (D360a stays). (h) Round 3: a judge error is excluded only if every gated slice holding it is within the cap (else filled everywhere, `overall` included); slices are listed from all shared cases (a slice without baseline values shows `INSUFFICIENT_DATA`); baseline `error` beyond 20 % of a gated slice is a `GateInputError`; the last attempt per case decides whether a request failed and a response-less record with `error_rate` 0 is a `GateInputError`. T1.10 follow-ups: cross-check `directions` against `MetricRegistry`; check the hard-fail NA reasons for robust_pr / retrieve-mode cases. (f) Regenerated `schemas/pruefstand_config.schema.json` (docstring drift from T1.6) together with the new `gate_report.schema.json`. |
| D361 | [T1.8, run id] A run id is `<UTC yyyymmddTHHMMSS>-<6 hex>` (`store.artifacts.new_run_id`); `RunManifest.run_id` and the run directory name are the same string | [0020](adr/0020-run-directory-lossless-store.md) |
| D362 | [T1.8, atomic writes] `ArtifactStore.write_run` fills a hidden sibling `.<run_id>.<hex>.tmp` directory and `os.replace`s it into place (an existing run directory is never overwritten; a failure removes the temp dir); `mark_aborted` / `add_counts` write `run.json.tmp` and `os.replace` it, re-validated with `RunManifest` | [0020](adr/0020-run-directory-lossless-store.md) |
| D363 | [T1.8, interface change] A run directory gains `scored.jsonl` (one `ScoredCase` per line), the lossless source of `RunResults.values`: `results.parquet` has only the 13 columns and no `TestCase` body or metric details. `write_run` also takes an optional keyword `llm_calls: pl.DataFrame` (written as `llm_calls.parquet`). T6.1 baselines and T8.1 sanitising must carry `scored.jsonl` | [0020](adr/0020-run-directory-lossless-store.md) |
| D364 | [T1.8, interface change] `llm.budget.tally_charges()` / `ChargeTally`: a context-variable scope counting what `Budget.charge` takes in it; the runner releases exactly `2 - used` per judge call (R9 X4.5) and reports `RunManifest.cost` (judge charges only; `gpu_seconds` 0) from it. `Budget` behaviour is unchanged | [0020](adr/0020-run-directory-lossless-store.md) |
| D365 | [T1.8, no new dependency] `DiskKVCache` is a stdlib `sqlite3` store (`<dir>/<namespace>/cache.sqlite3`, table `kv(key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, value BLOB)`), values are opaque bytes and never unpickled. `diskcache` 5.6.3 was tried first and dropped: pip-audit reports PYSEC-2026-2447 (pickle deserialisation) and the cache sits next to untrusted target data; no audit ignore was added | [0020](adr/0020-run-directory-lossless-store.md) |
| D366 | [T1.8, scope notes] (a) Target-cache entries are JSON `{mode, latency_ms, response}`; an entry of the other mode is a miss, errors are never cached, a failing cache write only logs. (b) Retrieve-mode records compute `context_recall`, `ndcg_at_k`, `tenant_leak` and the record metrics; every other answer metric is `not_applicable` (reason `retrieve_mode` or `not_in_judged_subset`). After a `/v1/retrieve` 404 fallback the case is scored as an answer case. (c) Judged values without a call carry `details.reason`: `category`, `not_in_judged_subset`, `retrieve_mode`, `no_judges`, `hard_fail`, `judge_quota`, `judge_cap`; only the last two count in `judge_skipped`. (d) `counts`: `ok` / `error` / `cached` / `retrieve` / `retrieve_fallback` count cases; `not_applicable` counts metric values; new `metric_truncated` (deterministic) next to `judge_truncated` counts values whose `details.truncated` is true (also logged). (e) Deterministic metrics run synchronously on the event loop, one case at a time, so their shared wall-clock regex budget is not eaten by concurrency. (f) `judged_cases` counts cases where a judge call was made (success or `LLMError`); `judge_degraded` = `judge_error / judged_cases > 0.05` | – |
| D367 | [T1.8, review hardening] `read_run` fails closed and leaks nothing: run files must be regular non-symlink files read with a bound (128 MiB, `lstat` + `O_NOFOLLOW`), case ids unique, `results.parquet` checked by columns and footer row count before it is read, errors built from `exc.errors()` without input values. `DiskKVCache`: namespace validated, key/value types checked, values capped at 16 MiB (bigger rows are misses). A target response with a lone surrogate becomes a target error (it could not be stored or written); `write_run` maps ValueError/UnicodeError to `StoreError` and removes its tmp dir; stale `.<run>.<8 hex>.tmp` dirs are removed by name and newest mtime. No core model changed | [0020](adr/0020-run-directory-lossless-store.md) |
| D368 | [T1.8, round-4 review; replaces the rejected footer-only text] `RunManifest.artifact_sha256` (new field, interface change) holds the SHA-256 of `results.parquet`, `responses.jsonl` and `scored.jsonl`, set by `write_run`. `read_run` stream-hashes each file (regular non-symlink file, size cap, then hash) and raises `StoreIntegrityError` (new subclass of `StoreError`) on a mismatch or missing entry, before any parser sees it; `results.parquet` is never parsed by `read_run` (a hostile footer aborts polars). Error texts show only model field names, never run ids or keys from files. `DiskKVCache`: blob-only rows, key length cap 512, the constructor raises `StoreError` for a corrupt or locked cache (T1.10: no cache). The runner parses cache blobs with one typed model, treats any failure as a miss, validates unicode on hits and skips a failing cache for the rest of the run | [0020](adr/0020-run-directory-lossless-store.md). Threat model: the hashes sit in `run.json` next to the files, so they protect against corruption, partial or accidental edits and planted parquet bombs, not against forgery; T6.1, T8.1 and `RunIndex` must hash and parse the same in-memory bytes (`pl.read_parquet(BytesIO(data))`), never re-open the parquet by name after hashing; `write_run` refuses a parquet over the read cap | [0020](adr/0020-run-directory-lossless-store.md) |
| D369 | [T0.7 fix, vm_check] `firewall` accepts either Docker chain set: Docker >= 28 (`DOCKER`, `DOCKER-FORWARD`, `DOCKER-BRIDGE`, `DOCKER-CT`, `DOCKER-INTERNAL`; real VM: Docker 29.1.3) or the legacy `DOCKER-ISOLATION-STAGE-1/2` + `DOCKER`; it still FAILs when `DOCKER-USER` is missing, our rules are not in it, or neither set is present. `gh-scope` tests the token, not the owner role: `gh api repos/<public>/actions/permissions` must answer 403 "Resource not accessible by personal access token" (PASS); 200 = FAIL; anything else = WARN. `pruefstand-firewall.sh` never relied on the isolation chains. (Number may be renumbered by a later merge: PR #21 takes D361-D368.) |
| D370 | [T2.4] `parallel_id` is `<celex>:<block id>`: `32016R0679:art-6` for an Article, `32016R0679:art-6:abs-2` for paragraph 2 of an Article split by paragraph, `32016R0679:rec-1-5` for a block of recitals (`rec-10` for a lone one); German chunk and English twin carry the same value. `ArticleChunker` splits an Article when its body has more than 1 500 tokens **and** the body starts with the marker of paragraph 1 (`1.` or `(1)`, numbers without gaps; an Article that opens with an unnumbered sentence is a list of definitions and is never split); `chunk_aligned` splits an Article in both languages when it is long in either, and raises `DatasetError` when the chunk ids still differ. T2.5 must call `chunk_aligned` for every (DE, EN) pair (not `ArticleChunker.chunk` per document) and treat its `DatasetError` as a misaligned act; the CLI does so and subtracts such an act from `acts`. `citation_key` stays the Article key (`Art. 6 DSGVO`) for all pieces, the piece is named by `section="Art. 6 Abs. 1"`. | – |
| D371 | [T2.4] EUR-Lex `Document.text` is a block format (record-separator marker line `\x1eART 6\tTitle` / `\x1eREC 12\t`, then the body lines); all control characters are removed from the source text, so input cannot forge a marker. One `Document` per (act, language), `version_date` = Official Journal publication date, `metadata` = `celex`, `citation_en`, `attribution`, `layout` (`eli`, `flat`, `legacy`), `content_hash`. Three layouts are read because the pinned CELLAR files differ: 18 acts use ELI divs (`art_N`, `rct_N`), 2002/58/EC and 2003/88/EC are plain `<p>` lines; unprefixed `ti-art` and flat siblings are supported and tested. A DE/EN pair with different Article or recital sets is skipped and counted (`misaligned`), never half-loaded. | – |
| D372 | [T2.4] 16 EU acts added to `law_aliases.yaml` under `eu:` (DMA, Data Act, DGA, ePrivacy-RL, CRA, DORA, eIDAS-VO, Strombinnenmarkt-RL/-VO, EED, REMIT, Whistleblower-RL, VRRL, ProdHaft-RL, Arbeitszeit-RL, Arbeitsbedingungen-RL); two-letter abbreviations that are German words (`DA`) are avoided because the parser matches case-insensitively; `ingest/eurlex_acts.py` maps CELEX to abbreviation and English label, `test_eurlex_citation_keys.py` checks both against the YAML and `data/sources.yaml`. | – |
| D373 | [T2.4] EUR-Lex HTML is untrusted: 4 MiB size cap before reading, strict UTF-8, lxml HTML parser without network/DTD/entity expansion, caps on nesting depth (200) and element count (400 000), iterative linear extraction (an Article stops at the next sibling Article heading in every layout, nested Article/recital divs contribute no text of their own block, footnote references are emptied in place, and extracted text is capped at 8 M characters per act); tests assert 4x input takes at most 8x time. Follow-up from T1.5a: the `am`/`an` Article-number suffix deny-list is not revisited here because none of the 20 pinned acts has a lettered Article number with those suffixes (CRR, where they occur, is not in the corpus). | – |
| D374 | [T1.13, slice corpus] `corpus/slice_v1` is the one committed corpus (about 50 KB, `.gitignore` re-includes it): KSchG §§ 1-4, ArbZG §§ 3-5, BGB §§ 620-626, BDSG § 26 (GII) and DSGVO Art. 5-6 in German and English (EUR-Lex via `scripts/slice/eurlex_min.py`, replaced by T2.4; `parallel_id` `32016R0679:art-<n>`), plus 6 hand-written private documents (3 each for `versicherung_nord` and `kanzlei_mueller`, token and semantic canaries in `canaries.public.json`) and 4 planted injection documents (`PWNED-<4 hex>`, plain, table cell, footnote, mixed DE/EN). A § above 350 tokens is split by Absatz (`ParagraphChunker(max_tokens=350)`; e5 reads 512 tokens). `corpus_version` = `slice-<latest pin date>.<8 hex of the four file hashes>` (current value in `corpus/slice_v1/manifest.json`); the builder is deterministic (`created_at` = the pin date). The planted instruction lines are phrased as a binding "Antwort-Vorgabe" because a 0.5B model ignores a plain "ignore previous instructions" |
| D375 | [T1.13, tier-1 model pins; the emergency fallback of the prompt is used] Generator `Qwen/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct-GGUF` Q4_K_M (revision 9217f5db, sha256 74a4da8c..., Apache-2.0, family `qwen`), embedding `intfloat/multilingual-e5-small` (revision 614241f6, MIT, `query: `/`passage: `, 512 tokens, 384 dims; TEI loads `onnx/model.onnx`, sha256 ca456c06...), TEI image `cpu-1.8` (digest in `docker-compose.yml`), llama.cpp `b12019`. Why the fallback: on the 4-thread CPU (virtualised Haswell, about 64 prompt tokens/s and 12 generated tokens/s for a 1.5B model) the 1.5B and 1.7B candidates cannot meet p95 <= 15 s with 4 x 256-token chunks (about 1 000 prompt tokens). Measured with `scripts/models/select_candidate.sh` (10 smoke questions cited, p95 over 20 distinct first-time cases, wall time of those 20, `<think>` blocks, planted injections followed with the guard on / off): **qwen2.5-0.5b-instruct-q4_k_m 7/10, p95 14.2 s, 2.6 min, 0, 0/4, 1/4** (load average 4.5); qwen2.5-1.5b-instruct-q4_k_m **5/10, p95 36.8 s, 5.6 min, 0, 0/4, 0/4** (load average 4.3); qwen3-1.7b-q4_k_m (unsloth Q4_K_M, thinking off via `chat_template_kwargs`, `<think>` count 0) **7/10, p95 35.6 s, 6.2 min, 0, 0/4, 0/4** (load average 4.3). Both fail p95 <= 15 s and the guard-off injection acceptance (>= 1 of 4 followed); the 1.5B model also fails 6/10 cited. The second CPU type for the injection acceptance (AI9-13): the deferral is accepted by the owner, conditional on the explicit T1.12 Done-when item "0/4 injections followed (guard on) on two CPU types, hosted runner and a second CPU type"; until then it was measured on one CPU only |
| D376 | [T1.13, dependencies] `apps/rag-demo` adds `fastapi`, `uvicorn`, `httpx`, `pydantic`, `pydantic-settings`, `pyyaml`, `numpy` (serving) and the extra `index` = `jsonschema` (corpus rows are validated against `schemas/chunk.schema.json`); the root dev group adds `testcontainers` (Qdrant and TEI integration fixtures). Qdrant is spoken to through its REST API with `httpx`, so no `qdrant-client` |
| D377 | [T1.13, scope notes, no interface change] (a) `/v1/info.models` keys are `generator`, `generator_family`, `embedding` (the prompt's `embed` is the interfaces.md/OpenAPI `embedding`). (b) `test_openapi.py` compares the generated and the checked-in OpenAPI by structure (paths, operation ids, schema refs, property names and types, required, `additionalProperties`), ignores descriptions and examples, and leaves out `/v1/retrieve` (T3.5) and `stats.prompt_overflow` (T3.6). (c) `make cassettes-contract` records `query_ok`, `query_refused`, `query_other_tenant` (from the `tenant_filter_post_topk` instance), `info`, `health`; `query_no_chunks`, `query_chunks_no_tenant` (shapes rag-demo never emits) and `retrieve_ok` (T3.5) keep `source: openapi-example`. | D379 | [T0.6, fix] `probe.sh` finds the Ollama binary from the process serving :11434 (ss, lsof, fuser, else a scan of own processes whose argv is `ollama serve` under `PROBE_PROC_ROOT`, default /proc; fallback `command -v`) because a non-interactive ssh has a minimal PATH; a pid counts only if we own it and its exe is a regular executable file named `ollama`. Every output line is filtered: the binary path (`<ollama-bin>`), its install prefix (`<ollama-prefix>`), $HOME, its resolved path and the passwd home (`<home>`) on path-segment boundaries, and the login (`id -un`, $USER, $LOGNAME, case-insensitive, as path segment or before `@`) as `<cluster-user>`; the last filter fails closed (a line still holding one of these, also percent-encoded, or the login as a case-insensitive substring, as a whole word when shorter than 4 characters, becomes `CHECK redact FAIL leak suppressed`); stderr is filtered too; host names and addresses are not scrubbed. `model-load` (opt-in) checks HTTP status, `error` field, curl failure/timeout (`LOAD_TIMEOUT_S`) and `/api/ps`, validates `GEN_MODEL`, loads with keep_alive 5m and, once the request is sent, unloads with keep_alive 0 and confirms it through `/api/ps` (else "unload by hand"; keep_alive 5m bounds a missed unload if the probe is killed) |
| D380 | [T1.9] The gate result is a **commit status**, not a check run: `GitHubPublisher.post_status` is the only call of the statuses API (state allow-list `pending|success|failure|error`, description cut to 140 characters, context `pruefstand/gate` unless given) and `set_check` is a thin wrapper that maps `(status, no_baseline, infra_error)` through the interfaces §10 table. For `no_baseline` the caller passes the `eval_fingerprint` as `summary` (description `no baseline for <fp>; run baseline-promote`). `GitHubPublisher` only talks to an `https://` API, validates `repo` (`owner/name`), `sha` (hex) and the token (printable ASCII, no white space), never follows redirects, retries 5xx and transport errors 3 times, raises `PublishError` (a `PruefstandError` holding endpoint and status code only, never a response body or the token) and hides the token from `repr`. |
| D381 | [T1.9] The PR comment renders target- and branch-controlled text (case ids, slice values, git refs, evidence, unknown metric or reason names), so every such string passes `md_escape` / `md_code` / `safe_url` (`report/format.py`): control and bidi characters removed, line breaks folded to spaces, length capped (200), `\| ` ` * ~ [ ] ( ) ! $` and `_` outside words backslash-escaped, `< > &` HTML-escaped, `@`, `#<digit>`, `://` and `www.` broken with a zero-width space (no mention, issue link, autolink, image or raw HTML), code spans cannot be closed from inside. Hard-fail evidence is additionally masked with the canary pattern (defence in depth, the gate masks first). Only `http(s)` report URLs without white space, `<`, `>` or quotes become a link. All passes are single linear regex passes. |
| D382 | [T1.9] Size guard of the PR comment (60 000 characters): lists are capped on the first pass (1 000 slice rows, 25 items per line, 50 hard-fail rows, 50 ids); if the result is still too long the passes repeat with 20 slice rows / 10 items / 10 fails and then with no slice rows; the last resort is a hard cut at a line end that closes open `<details>` blocks. Whenever anything was cut the comment ends with `… truncated, see full report`. The counts in headings (`Hard fails (n)`, `Gated slices not passing (n)`) always show the true totals. |
| D383 | [T1.9] Display choices where ui-spec examples and rules differ: seconds are zero-padded (`14m 03s`, `0m 41s`, `18m 12s`); tokens follow the rule `4.1 M` / `812 k` / `< 1 k` (so 200 000 tokens read `200 k`, the §12.2 example was corrected to match); a `UNSUPPORTED_CONFIG` report is titled `⚠️ WARN` with the quote line "Unsupported configuration — counted as WARN."; hard-fail rows show the metric code in the Rule column (`rule` is `hard_fail` or `hard_fail_unverifiable`, the latter is marked "(unverifiable)"); `Likely cause: mixed` drops the `: <codes>` tail because `GateReport` does not carry the matched codes. |
| D384 | [T1.9] Known gaps handed to later tasks: (1) the judge-truncation notice shows the count from `RunManifest.counts["judge_truncated"]` only; the case ids need `RunResults`, which `render_pr_comment(report, base, cand, *, report_url)` does not receive (an additive keyword such as `judge_truncated_case_ids` is a follow-up for T1.10/T6.4); (2) `GateReport.absolute_checks` also holds non-gated per-slice rows without `informational_reason`; by the §11.1 one-table rule they render on the "Informational" line with reason `–` (capped at 25 items); the gate should give them a reason or move them to `notable_slices`. |
| D385 | [T1.9, review round 1] Canary masking in `report` runs inside `_clean` (used by `md_escape` / `md_code`, so for every untrusted string), BEFORE the control characters are removed: `mask_canaries` folds the text like `security_normalise` (NFKD, `IGNORABLE` and Mn dropped, case fold, `CONFUSABLES` look-alikes, letters and digits kept), finds `canary` + two letters + >= 6 hex digits, the hex run never swallowing the next `canary` with any separators and replaces that span of the original text by `CANARY-**-******` (after six digits the hex run stops at a white-space gap). The gate's `redact_evidence` has the same gaps (also adjacent tokens, look-alikes, fillers): T1.7b; `write_gate_json` does no masking. |
| D386 | [T1.9, review round 1] Publisher: the comment POST is never retried (a 5xx may hide a created comment); PATCH and the status POST are. `api_url` must be `https://api.github.com` (no user info, port, query) or `https://<enterprise_host>` for the explicit keyword `enterprise_host` (interface addition, default `None`). `bot_login="pruefstand-bot[bot]"` is required for the App-based executor (T6.2a): `GET /user` answers 403 for installation tokens and the fallback is `github-actions[bot]`. A comment search that hits the 100-page cap logs `comment_search_page_cap`. |
| D387 | [T1.9, review round 1] Reason-less rows of `absolute_checks` (per-slice rows with `gated=False`) are omitted from the PR comment's Informational line (they would crowd out the reasoned and judged rows); with `notable=True` they appear on the Notable line as `<metric> · dimension=value <value>`. This is a fourth ui-spec §11.1 surface omission. Tokens in the ui-spec §12.2 example corrected to `200 k` (D383). `GH-123` autolinks are neutralised like `#123`. |
| D388 | [T0.6, ops] T0.6 cluster feasibility verdict **go** (owner, 2026-11-02; `summarise.py`: go-with-conditions, treated as go): one Ollama server, 2 × A40, detached process survives logout, tunnel + `think:false` + `num_ctx` verified, outbound HTTPS for pulls works, cold load 8 s from the cluster disk. Conditions: `bge-m3` not pulled yet (T3.1b) and the shared `/data` has only 14.1 GB free against 11.2 GB needed for the pulls (a known risk: T3.1b checks free space right before each pull and asks the owner instead of pulling when it is below the need). Evidence: `infra/cluster/feasibility/probe-2026-11-02.out` with the models directory shown as `<models-dir>`, the binary as `<ollama-bin>`; T3.1a is unblocked. |
| D389 | [T0.6 fix] `probe.sh` replaces the value of `MODELS_DIR` by `<models-dir>` in every output line (header, `ollama-store`, `models-space`, `model-load`), first in the redaction list and on the same path-segment boundary as binary, prefix and home, before the fail-closed filter; the former visible `/data/<login>/Models` form is gone, the login filter is unchanged. `summarise.py` takes one line per check id: the last real result wins and a `SKIP` line never overrides a measured one; table, conditions and verdict use the winning lines only (the concatenated probe, `MEASURE_LOAD` and tunnel outputs). Supersedes the worst-status-wins rule for duplicate ids |
| D390 | [T4.4] `numbers` rewrites a German number only when the reading is unambiguous: thousands-grouped with a decimal part, with two or more groups, or with a unit (`%`, `€`, `EUR`, `Euro`, `Prozent`) next to it; a plain decimal comma only with such a unit; `Abs. 2,3`, `Nr. 1,2`, `§ 622`, integers < 1000 and dates stay. `€` always becomes `EUR` (`250€` → `250 EUR`, `€ 99,90` → `EUR 99.90`). If any German-format number would remain after the rewrite (`zwischen 1.000 und 5.000 €`, `Abs. 2,3 und 13,7 %`, `13,375 %`) the whole text stays unchanged (no half-converted sentence; review of PR #29) |
| D391 | [T4.4] `dates` rewrites `d.m.yyyy` only when it is a valid calendar date (`31.02.2026`, `00.10.2026` unchanged) and not part of a longer dotted token (`1.000.2026`, `1.2.3`). `citation_style` rewrites `§` (national laws only) and `Art.` with up to six qualifiers (Abs./S./Nr./lit. spelled out) and a law from `law_aliases.yaml`; the genitive phrase of a law is its alias that starts with `des`/`der` (one per law, 31 national + 20 EU rows added; no second law-name map); `§§`, unknown laws and `§` + EU act and a law followed by `-` (`DSGVO-konform`) stay unchanged; dates need a year 1900-2100 and are skipped after `§`/`Az.`/`Nr.`/`Aktenzeichen` or before `-<digit>` |
| D392 | [T4.4] `typos` makes one edit in one word of >= 6 letters: deletion, adjacent transposition of two different letters, or substitution by a neighbouring QWERTZ key (case kept); the first letter is never edited; letter runs over 40 characters are not words (bounded work per edit); words that are a single-word law name or alias (full multi-word names are not protected) or have a capital after the first letter (`KSchG`, `DSGVO`) are skipped; edit distance 1 is Damerau-Levenshtein (a transposition counts 1). The edit type is drawn uniformly among the available types, then uniformly among its variants, all from the passed `Generator` |
| D393 | [T4.4] Every deterministic perturbation NFC-normalises its input and output; `changed` compares against the NFC form of the input, so a decomposed `u\u0308` alone is not a change. `perturb_case` refuses an already perturbed case (`DatasetError`) and copies every other field of the base case, including `block` and `judged_subset` (T4.1 decides those for the variants) |
| D394 | [T4.4] `typos` skips every whitespace-delimited token containing `@`, `://`, `www.`, a dotted domain, an uppercase run + `-`/`_` or a hex tail (URLs, e-mail, canary-like tokens). `abbreviations` adds the space `i.V.m.§ 1` needs. Known limits, left for T4.4a: capitalised `Z. B.` is not expanded; all-caps words with umlauts transliterate to `AeRGER`; `10.000,- €` and `3,5 Mio. €` are only partly handled; `1.000 Euro` keeps `Euro` while `€` becomes `EUR` |
| D400 | [T0.5, ops] T0.5 repo settings applied 2026-11-02: squash-only merges, auto-delete branches, ruleset on main (PR required, 0 approvals, conversation resolution, linear history, no force push or deletion, bypass = Repository admin for PRs only, "up to date" off), required checks `check` and `scan`, read-only default workflow permissions, Actions cannot approve PRs, Pages source = GitHub Actions, labels created; the pruefstand-bot App and the gate-router environment come later with T6.2a/T6.2b (T0.5 S7a/S10). D66 is superseded in part for the `docs / build` and `build-images / build` names. |

Unexpected exceptions are reported by type only; a traceback only with `--verbose`. `validate dataset` reports an invalid dataset as 2. |

| D413 | [T3.1a] Disk rule (owner, binding): `fetch-model.sh` reads the free space of the models disk from `status.sh` right before every pull and stops with the **new exit code 17** (message "free space first: swap rule ... ask the owner") when it is below model size + `MODELS_MIN_FREE_GB` (2 GB); the cluster-side `ollama-op.sh pull` refuses (17) below the 2 GB reserve as a second guard. The `pruefstand-cluster` op list is unchanged. |
| D415 | [T3.1a] `deploy-cluster-bin.sh` talks to the cluster with plain ssh, so it runs as the service user; the owner runs `make gpu-deploy-bin`, which copies `infra/gpu` and `infra/cluster` to a world-readable temp dir and runs `sudo -u pruefstand <tmp>/gpu/deploy-cluster-bin.sh`. A user other than `pruefstand` (or `--bin-dev`) writes `pruefstand-bin-dev/`. No `put` op was added to the wrapper: that would let the owner run arbitrary scripts with the cluster key. Confused-deputy guard (review M3): `make gpu-deploy-bin` and `make gpu-install-wrapper` run a `git archive origin/main` export, and the scripts themselves refuse a tree that differs from `origin/main` (`lib/git_verify.sh`); the installer never overwrites a file it did not create without `--replace` (diff first). `health.sh` exit 14 uses `status.sh` (`bin-integrity`, `bin-sums`) compared with `deploy-cluster-bin.sh --print-sums`, again without a new op. |
| D416 | [T3.1a] Wrapper hardening (review M1, M2): the mutating ops need the GPU lock held by an ANCESTOR of the wrapper (`/proc/locks` inode match, PPid chain through `sudo`); tags must be in the allow-list `/usr/local/lib/pruefstand-cluster.allow` generated at install time from `models.yaml`; `pull` takes its size from that list and `ollama-op.sh pull <tag> <bytes>` checks bytes + reserve on the models filesystem (unreadable free space is exit 17); `rm` needs `MAINTENANCE` (exit 3). `infra/vm/pruefstand-lock` (T0.7) changed: it now runs `flock -w N -E 75 <lock> <cmd>` instead of taking fd 9 and exec-ing, so the process that holds the lock stays an ancestor of `<cmd>` (the kernel records the pid of the short-lived `flock` child otherwise, which is dead by then and reusable); same exit codes 2/64/75; the owner reinstalls it if `/usr/local/bin` holds a copy (`sudo infra/vm/setup-users.sh`). The wrapper matches device and inode in `/proc/locks`. Preflight `health.sh` exit 14 is what verifies the cluster scripts' integrity before they are trusted. `health.sh` exit priority 16, 11, 12, 14, 13, 15, 10. A mode model that is not resident and a second `ollama serve` are exit 11; a catalogue model outside the mode is a WARN row (`--repair` unloads it), a non-catalogue model a WARN row (left alone); `--repair` progress goes to stderr so stdout is the CSV only. Free VRAM threshold `PRUEFSTAND_MIN_FREE_VRAM_MIB` (default 1024). |

No match is a WARN `gpu=unknown`, two cards a WARN `gpu=0+1`; `health.sh` turns the latter into exit 15. To be confirmed on the real server in S8 (fallback: the offload lines of `ollama.log`). |
| D418 | [T3.1a] Preload is `POST /api/generate {model, keep_alive, options}` for every model as specified; if the server refuses it for an embedding-only model (`bge-m3`), the script falls back to `POST /api/embed` with `input: ["."]` and the same options. Whether the fallback is ever used is recorded in S8. |

The test scores were recorded from the real model on the VM (`infra/gpu/rerank/record_scores.py`). Review fixes: every package is pinned with hashes (`requirements.in` compiled with `uv pip compile --generate-hashes`, installed with `--require-hashes`), the weights also by the sha256 of `model.safetensors` (checked at load), query capped at 2000 characters, 30 s socket timeout, negative `Content-Length` refused; the first start needs egress to huggingface.co, then `RERANK_OFFLINE=1`. The recorded image digest is the final pin; recorded when the owner builds it ([DOCKER] item). |
