| D431 | [T2.5] The tenant plan is data, not code: `corpus.yaml` holds `plan.rules`; a rule matches by exact `Document.source` plus optional `law_abbrev` and `metadata` equality (all given criteria), rules must be disjoint (a document matching two rules is a `ConfigError` even when both name the same tenant), a document matching none is a `ConfigError` naming its `doc_id` unless `default_tenant` is set (the repo plan sets none). OLDP decisions are routed by `metadata` (`jurisdiction: Arbeitsgericht` -> `kanzlei_mueller`, `tenant_domain: insurance` -> `versicherung_nord`, `tenant_domain: other` -> `shared_public`); a test pins every one of the 30 GII laws and 20 CELEX ids to exactly one rule |
| D432 | [T2.5] `corpus_version = <YYYY-MM-DD>.<8 hex>` is computed only by `core.fingerprint.corpus_version`; ingest passes `--date` (default today, UTC). `created_at` is midnight UTC of that date, so `manifest.json` and the chunk files are byte-identical for identical inputs and `diff -r` of two builds is empty. `write_corpus` recomputes the version from the content and refuses a mismatch |
| D433 | [T2.5] `write_corpus(chunks, out_dir, corpus_version, *, sources=None)`: the keyword-only `sources` (source id to pinned version) is an additive extension of the interfaces.md signature, because the manifest needs it and the signature had no other way to receive it. Files are written to a `.<name>.partial` staging directory, re-hashed, then renamed; an existing version directory must hold exactly the same bytes (versions are immutable); a symlinked output directory or parent is refused; lines are compact sorted-key JSON and `U+2028`, `U+2029`, `U+0085` are written as escapes so `str.splitlines()` readers see one record per line |
| D434 | [T2.5] Tenant isolation guards (security property): tenant ids match `[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,62}` and are not `unassigned` (they become file names and filter values); a loader-set tenant is refused; one `doc_id` is never assigned twice or to two tenants; a `chunk_id` occurs once; the writer re-checks all three; `pruefstand ingest` fails when a declared tenant gets no chunk. Chunks without a licence, or from a `memerag` source, are refused |
| D435 | [T2.5] The manifest `sources` come from `data/sources.yaml` (`pinned` for GII/EUR-Lex, `revision` for the OLDP dump). A loaded source without a pin is a `ConfigError`; documents from the `_extra_documents` hook (T2.6, T4.6) get the pin `synthetic`, must carry a tenant of the plan and a `doc_id` unused by public documents, and cannot claim a `gii:`, `eurlex:` or `oldp` source; extras with tenant `shared_public` are refused (visible to every tenant: private canary and attack documents never go there). The hook is a stub: T2.6 and T4.6 must extend it (T4.6 expects `manifest.sources["attacks"] = attack_set_version`, while the pin is forced to `synthetic` here; recorded as T2.5a). `max_chunks` also counts extras |
| D436 | [T2.5] Loader problems fail the build (`max_problems`, default 0): missing laws and acts are detected through the loaders' `expected` lists derived from the plan, unsafe archives and unparsable records are counted problems, and nothing is written. `max_chunks` (default 2 000 000) bounds the in-memory build. Ingest does not re-hash the raw files; `make data-verify` is that check and the pins only label provenance |
| D437 | [T2.5] `verify_corpus` (`ingest/corpus_verify.py`) checks a written directory without trusting it: manifest capped at 1 MiB, file set equals the manifest, regular files only (`O_NOFOLLOW`), sha256 is checked before a line is parsed, lines are capped at 4 MiB, each line must be a `Chunk` of the file's tenant, counts and `corpus_version` must agree. `scripts/validate_corpus.py` adds the check of every line against `schemas/chunk.schema.json` and prints `OK n=<N>` |
| D438 | [T2.5] `core.config_loader.load_model(path, Model)` is the generic form of `load_config` (same hardening: regular file, 1 MB, no aliases, no duplicate keys, depth 32); `load_config` delegates to it, `corpus.yaml` is loaded with it |
| D441 | [T6.1, interface] A baseline directory `<fp>/<ref>/` also holds `scored.jsonl.zst` (zstd of `scored.jsonl`): `results.parquet` has no `TestCase` body or metric details (D363), so `get()` could not rebuild `RunResults.values` from the round-9 file list. `manifest.json` (schema 1: run_id, eval_fingerprint, ref, promoted_at, git_sha, SHA-256 per stored file) pins the four stored files; `run.json` carries `artifact_sha256` of the uncompressed data files. `history/<run_id>/` keeps `run.json`, `results.parquet`, `manifest.json` only. `scored.jsonl.zst` holds full case bodies (forbidden strings, canaries): same sensitivity as `responses.jsonl.zst`, VM data root and encrypted backup only, never exported | D157, D209, D363 | [0004](adr/0004-baseline-store-git-branch.md) |
| D442 | [T6.1, security] Reading a baseline hashes and parses the same in-memory bytes (no re-open by name after hashing): `read_bounded` (regular file, no symlink, size cap), `manifest.json` SHA-256 (and, for the git store, the SHA-256 pinned in `index.json`), then the zstd frame is inflated with a hard cap (decompression bomb), `run.json` `artifact_sha256` is checked, and only then the JSONL is parsed. `results.parquet` is hashed but never parsed. Fingerprints, refs and run ids come from data and must match `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,63}` (refs: the seven fixed names); `<root>/<fp>`, `<ref>`, `history` and `history/<run_id>` must be real directories (symlinks refused). `_run_files.read_results` now shares `parse_results` / `require_hashes` with the baseline reader (messages unchanged) | D368 | [0004](adr/0004-baseline-store-git-branch.md) |
| D443 | [T6.1] Promotion is two-phase: the new baseline is staged as `<ref>.<hex>.partial` (fsync), swapped in with `os.rename` while the old one is kept as `<ref>.<hex>.old`, `history/<run_id>` is renamed in, and only then (git store) the one-file index commit is pushed (3 attempts, fresh clone each); a failure at any step rolls the data root back to the previous baseline, a success deletes the `.old` copy. A `get` that finds `<ref>.<hex>.old` without `<ref>` raises (interrupted promotion), never `None`. `put` itself refuses incomplete or flagged runs (`BaselineRefused`, a `StoreError` the CLI maps to exit 1) and stores `main` only for `block is None`, `nightly-b<k>`/`release-b<k>` only for `block == k`; `JUDGE_TRUNCATED_SHARE = 0.05` duplicates the gate's constant (`store` cannot import `pipeline`; a test keeps them equal). The push token is read from `PRUEFSTAND_BASELINES_TOKEN` (name configurable) and reaches git only through `GIT_ASKPASS` | D211, D224, D321 | [0004](adr/0004-baseline-store-git-branch.md) |
| D444 | [T6.1] `baseline promote --ref release-b<k>` projects the release run: cases without a block plus cases of block k, `block = k`, `stratum = "all"`, `n_cases` and the per-case counters (`ok`, `error`, `cached`, `retrieve`, `not_applicable`, `judge_truncated`, `metric_truncated`, `refusal_flag_mismatch`, `judged_cases`) recounted, `eval_fingerprint` recomputed with `core.fingerprint.eval_fingerprint(block=k)` from the manifest fields and the (single) corpus version of the kept cases. Run-level counters (`model_reloads`, `upstream_errors`, judge errors, ...) cannot be split by case and are dropped; `cost` and `duration_s` stay those of the release run. The `run_id` stays the release run's id (the three projections live under three fingerprints) | D321 | [0004](adr/0004-baseline-store-git-branch.md) |
| D445 | [T6.1] `index.json` (schema 1) maps fingerprint -> ref -> runs (oldest first, newest = current: run_id, manifest_sha256 of the `<ref>/manifest.json`, promoted_at, git_sha). Retention (`prune_baselines(root, keep, unused_days, now)`, disk only) is "last 20 per fingerprint across refs": the newest `keep` `history/<run_id>` per fingerprint by `promoted_at` (the history entry of every current baseline is pinned), fingerprints with no promotion for more than `unused_days` (measured by `promoted_at`, not mtime) lose everything but their `main` and `release-b<k>` directories (a quiet `main` baseline is never aged out, so the next PR does not fall to `no_baseline`), unreadable manifests and names that are not safe path components are left alone, symlinks are never followed; `GitBranchBaselineStore.prune` then reconciles the index with the disk in one commit (nothing to remove: no commit) | D110, D145 | [0004](adr/0004-baseline-store-git-branch.md) |
| D446 | [T6.1] Cache trust domains: `PRUEFSTAND_CACHE_MODE` is `overlay` (default; main's cache opened read-only under `PRUEFSTAND_CACHE_DIR` or `$PRUEFSTAND_HOME/cache`, writes into a per-job temp directory deleted afterwards, also on failure) or `shared` (one writable cache for main and nightly); anything else is a `ConfigError`. `pipeline/cache.target_cache()` wires it into `run_eval`; a cache that cannot be opened is "no cache" (an unreadable shared cache in overlay mode still gives the PR its own overlay). **Cache identity (review of PR 41):** a PR image is untrusted code and the cache key rests on what the target reports about itself, so (a) a PR job reads main's cache only when the executor sets `PRUEFSTAND_TRUSTED_TARGET_ID` (the image digest it pulled); that id is hashed into `target_fingerprint` and the cache key, the reported git sha/digest can only add to it; without it the PR gets its own empty overlay, never main's answers; (b) a target that reports git sha `unknown`/empty and no image digest (or no `/v1/info`) is opaque: no cache at all, neither read nor written; (c) `counts.cached_from_base` records answers served from main's cache; the gate refuses a candidate that has it for all its cases (`GateInputError`: the candidate never ran), downgrades a PASS with some to WARN, and the PR comment shows "N of M answers served from cache (baseline overlay)" `build_cache(mode, shared, overlay, namespace)` also serves namespace `llm`, but no `LLMClient` exists in `run_eval` yet (judges arrive with T5.2a/b): that wiring is a follow-up of T5.1/T5.2b | D75 | [0006](adr/0006-two-caches-fingerprints.md) |
| D447 | [T6.1, T1.8a, T1.10a] `gate --from-store [--ref]` calls `pipeline.gate.gate_from_store`: unknown ref -> `StoreError`; `nightly-b<k>` / `release-b<k>` with a candidate of another block -> `GateInputError` (exit 3); a baseline of another stack (tier 1 vs tier 2) -> `GateInputError`; no baseline -> `no_baseline` report, exit 0, the CLI prints `no baseline for <fp>; run baseline-promote`. `--ref` without `--from-store`, or `--baseline` with `--from-store`, is exit 2. `run_gate` (both paths) logs `gate_config_hash_differs` when `stable_hash(cfg)` differs from `run.json` `config_hash` | D402, D403 | [0004](adr/0004-baseline-store-git-branch.md) |
| D448 | [T6.1, security] `sanitise_run` masks (case-insensitively) every canary token, fact and forbidden answer of the registry plus every `forbidden_strings` value of the run as `‹redacted›` in answers, citations, errors, questions, reference answers and string metric details; case bodies keep their `forbidden_strings` count but redacted; chunk text is emptied; a case whose forbidden strings contain a canary (or are contained in one, from 6 characters) or whose question is a canary probe question is dropped; an empty registry is refused. `write_public_export` writes `run.json`, `results.parquet`, `responses.jsonl` and `manifest.json` (`sanitised: true`, SHA-256 per file) and never `scored.jsonl`; it refuses to write when any text still contains `canary-`. `seal_export` re-seals the manifest after T8.1 added files. `push_dashboard_data` validates the export before any network access (marker, exact file list, hashes, no symlink, no `CANARY-`) | D315 | [0011](adr/0011-dashboard-static-nextjs.md) |
| D449 | [T6.1, interface] `push_dashboard_data(src, repo, *, token_env, extra, remote=None)` and `push_heartbeat(repo, payload, *, token_env, remote=None)` gain the optional keyword `remote` (git URL or local path replacing `https://github.com/<repo>.git`; used by the tests with a bare repository; with it the size check is skipped). `src` is the flat export directory: top-level files go to `runs/<run_id>/`, its `cases/` subdirectory to `cases/<run_id>/`. The rewrite (`runs/index.json` rows with the `RunIndexEntry` fields copied from `run.json` / `gate.json`, newest 200 runs, `cases/` + `report.html` for the newest 20 by `created_at` then run id, `manifest.json`) follows D300; a run directory without a valid `run.json` is deleted. The 1 GB check uses the GitHub REST API through `httpx` (`size` in KB, token as a header) instead of `gh api`, fails closed above 1 GB and open when the size is unavailable. The heartbeat payload must be at most 64 snake_case fields with string values of at most 1000 characters | D300 | [0013](adr/0013-observability.md) |
| D450 | [T6.1, dependencies] `duckdb` (the `RunIndex` of ADR-0003: SQL over all runs) and `zstandard` (the `responses.jsonl.zst` / `scored.jsonl.zst` of the baseline layout, with a bounded decompressor) are added to `packages/pruefstand`; the stdlib has neither. `RunIndex` keeps tables `runs` and `results`, ingests a run only after `read_results` verified its hashes (rows come from `scored.jsonl`, never from a parquet on disk), skips runs that fail verification with a warning, and answers `query` on a read-only connection with `enable_external_access` off (no file read, no file write) | D13 | [0003](adr/0003-files-source-of-truth-duckdb.md) |
| D451 | [ops, owner decision 2026-11-02] The lead agent may create and remove `/var/lib/pruefstand/MAINTENANCE` and remove or swap Ollama models without asking the owner, only after the `:latest`/digest fix is merged and deployed, only models listed in `infra/gpu/models.yaml`, only through the wrapper (`make gpu-pull`/`gpu-rm`), under `pruefstand-lock`, only while no gate or nightly runs, with `MAINTENANCE` always removed again (also on failure), never removing the model the current mode uses without switching modes first and NEVER removing the generator `qwen3.8:27b-q8_0` (`GENERATOR_TAG`), not even to free space (if freeing space would need it, ask the owner), and every change logged in `gpu-cluster.md` section 8. `fetch-model.sh` exit 17 is no longer a stop (the lead may free space by removing catalogue models the task does not need; ask only when that is not enough). Task agents never do this. Supersedes the "never edit MAINTENANCE" part of CLAUDE.md section 1 rule 8 and the "never delete anything on /data" instruction of the S2b hand-off; changes the ADR-0019 swap sequence step 1 from owner to lead. |
| D452 | [T3.1a, measured 2026-11-02] On the real cluster: `gemma3:12b` pulled with the catalogue digest (free disk 12.7 GB before, 4.5 GB after); day and night load the same set; generator alone on GPU 0 (28 135 MiB used), `bge-m3` + `gemma3:12b` on GPU 1 (13 356 MiB); `make gpu-repair` after `stop-ollama` restarted exactly one detached server with the start script of record in 25 s, `env` matched `cluster.env` (NOPRUNE warning gone), day set resident; `rerank-cpu` healthy through `docker exec` (200) and the seven `gpu` tests pass. The `gpu` test for `/api/ps` compares names without the `:latest` suffix Ollama adds. `kv_bytes_per_token` stays a T3.1b measurement. |
| D470 | [T3.1b, phase 1, measured 2026-11-03 on the resident set] Benchmark code lives in `experiments/model_selection/` (native Ollama client, no `/v1`, no residency key; `think: false`; catalogue options; `make bench-models` under `pruefstand-lock`, refuses to run unless the day set is resident). Results: `results/2026-11-03.csv`. The generator `qwen3.8:27b-q8_0` does NOT batch: aggregate decode stays 14.4 tok/s at concurrency 1/4/8 (19.4 tok/s per stream, prefill about 970 tok/s, TTFT 30 s at 4 streams), so `budget.py` models it with `slots=1`; `gemma3:12b` scales (decode 57 tok/s per stream at 1 stream, aggregate incl. prefill 36.3 at 1 and 54.5 at 4, prefill 1 625 tok/s). Server env matches `cluster.env` (`OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=4`); why the generator behaves like one slot is open (load log needed) Judge JSON validity 50/50 with the schema, verdict accuracy 0.96. No reloads, no `<think>` in any answer | Measured; budgets in REPORT.md |
| D471 | [T3.1b, budget] With the measured rates (200 output tokens assumed; at num_predict 512 the GPU0 lane is about 175 min, see REPORT caveats) the PR tier is GPU0-bound: about 82 min for smoke 50 + core 300 generations (3 500-token prompts, 200 tokens out), GPU1 about 50 min, reranker lane 15.8 min at 1.2 s per query (assumed, CPU lane not measured yet); with the 150-case judged subset about 56 min. Nightly 600 generations about 139 min and 1 800 judge calls about 99 min, far below 480 min. The PR run fits 90 min without the subset but with only 8 min margin: pin `generation.max_tokens` and the prompt budget in `rag.gpu.yaml` before the first baseline (remedy a), the subset (remedy b) is the reserve. The reranker verdict (D222) stays open until the CPU lane is measured | Open: reranker lane, 384-token variant |
| D472 | [T3.1b, measured KV and context] `gemma3:12b` at `num_ctx` 12 288 x 4 slots uses 10.2 GB on GPU1 (13.4 GiB card use incl. `bge-m3`), `/api/ps` size grows by only about 7.9 KB per context token (sliding-window cache active, `swa_window` 1 024), far below the planning 196 608 B; qwen35 slope not reliable (11.9 KB per token if 4 slots, 47.6 KB if 1 slot). 20 faithfulness inputs of 9 146-9 220 prompt tokens: `prompt_eval_count` identical at 12 288 and 16 384 and identical verdicts (no silent truncation); rubric + question + answer allowance max 139 tokens (<= 3 072). So `num_ctx` 12 288 and `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL` 4 stay. `kv_bytes_per_token` in `models.yaml` is NOT changed yet: `/api/ps` sizes are Ollama estimates and not monotonic (gemma 12 288 > 16 384), the server log is not readable through the wrapper | Follow-up: wrapper op for the load log |

Match rule (`citations_match`): kind, number and law equal and gold `absatz` is `None` or equal to the prediction's; credit rule (`citation_credit`): 1.0 match, 0.5 same provision with gold Abs. set and prediction without, else 0.0 (SPEC §5, AI9-17) | `core/citations/model.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T1.5a | `core/data/law_aliases.yaml` is `version: 1` with two maps, `national:` and `eu:` (abbreviation → alias list; the abbreviation is always an alias). The split drives one rule: an English head (`Section`, `Sec.`, `s.`) is accepted only with a national law and then yields `para`. T2.2/T2.4/T4.4 append entries under the right map; an alias that names two laws raises `ConfigError` at load | `core/citations/aliases.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T1.5a | Parser conventions: number lists and ranges expand for every head (`Art. 17 und 18`, `Section 1 and 2`); qualifiers after a list belong to its last number; a head takes each qualifier at most once (a repeat ends the citation, which keeps the scan linear) and several values (`Abs. 2, 3`, `lit. a und b`, `lit. a–f`) keep the first; `UAbs.`/`Unterabs.`/`Halbsatz`/`Hs.`/`Alt.`/`Var.` are skipped; heads and keywords are case-insensitive except lowercase `s.` (head) vs `S.` (Satz); a law may be followed by `-konform`/`-Gesellschaft` compounds. A law-less citation borrows the next citation's law when joined by `i.V.m.` or by `und`/`sowie`/`,`; lending needs heads of the same kind in both cases, so `§ 622 und Art. 6 DSGVO` and `Art. 6 i.V.m. § 280 BGB` do not lend. `kind=para` exists only with a national law: `§ 6 DSGVO`, `§ 1 AI Act` and a lent `§ 622 DSGVO` are dropped. Numbers have at most 5 digits; ranges expand up to 50 members, longer ones keep both ends; one call returns at most `max(100, len(text) // 20)` citations (excess dropped in order, only the count is logged). Non-goal: `§ 1 BGB und KSchG` (a bare law after `und`). Dropped citations log only reason and offset at debug level. `core/citations` is a package (`model`, `aliases`, `parser`) so each file stays under 300 lines; the import path is unchanged | `core/citations/parser.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T1.5a | Linear-time tests use a scaling ratio, not a wall-clock bound: a 4x larger input must take at most 8x the base input (min of 3 `perf_counter` runs in one process; linear is ~4x, the quadratic bug ~16x); the only absolute bound is a 120 s hang guard. The amplification cap stays an exact length assertion in its own test | `tests/unit/core/test_citation_forms.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T1.5a (fix) | A § / Art. number takes a suffix of **one or two lowercase letters** (`5a`, `612a`, `53ca`): real laws have two-letter norms (KWG §§ 53ca–53cq, 17 norms) that the one-letter rule dropped as `uncitable` in the GII loader. Guards: `ff` is never a suffix (`§ 305ff.`, `§ 305 ff.`, `§ 305f.` keep their results), a suffix must end at a word boundary (three or more letters never match, so `§ 5abc`, `§ 622BGB` keep their results) and uppercase is never a suffix; the two-letter function words `am an da du er es im in ja je ob so um wo zu` are excluded so `Art. 6in` / `§ 6im` stay prose. **Known limits:** `am`/`an` are real CRR suffixes (Art. 325am/325an) but are excluded (heuristic; not reachable today, CRR is not in the alias table; revisit in T2.4 when EU acts are added), and prose such as `§ 5te` / `§ 3to` still parses as a suffix (recorded in `test_citation_suffix.py`). Scan remains linear (4x input ~4x time, incl. `§ 1ab ` x 20000). `Citation.number`, `normalise`, `citations_match` and `citation_credit` are unchanged. Follow-up for T2.2: the `uncitable` branch for these norms in the GII loader can be dropped once this and the loader are merged, and `KWG` must be in `law_aliases.yaml` | `core/citations/parser.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T1.2 | The model-family table is `packages/pruefstand/src/pruefstand/core/data/model_families.yaml` (keys `provider_prefixes`, `patterns`, `plan`), read once through `importlib.resources` by `core/models_family.py`; one provider prefix is stripped, first matching row wins, case-insensitive. The `gpt` row is `gpt|(^|/)o\d` (not `^o\d`) so `github/openai/o4-mini` matches after prefix stripping; the `plan:` block pins `judge_tier1: gpt` and `datagen: gpt` provisionally (T0.6 S4b / T4.2 S0 confirm) | `core/data/model_families.yaml`, [ADR-0009](adr/0009-litellm-providers.md) |
| 2026-11-01 | T1.2 | Config loading limits: files over 1 MB are refused and YAML aliases/anchors are refused outright (`yaml.parse` event scan before `safe_load`; a size limit alone does not stop a small alias bomb); no environment interpolation; all validation errors are reported at once, each with its YAML path. Cross-field rules raise `ConfigError` directly, single-field range errors are pydantic `ValidationError`s that `load_config` converts to `ConfigError` | `core/config_loader.py`, [ADR-0014](adr/0014-typed-config.md) |
| 2026-11-01 | T1.2 | **Interface note (review of PR #7):** `GatePolicy.gated_slices` default is derived from `slices` when omitted (`overall`, `perturbed_all`, plus `tenant`/`lang` only if in `slices`), stored as a tuple and always validated against `slices`, so `model_validate(model_dump())` round-trips and a custom `slices` never gates an uncomputed slice. Other accepted tightenings: `target.auth_env` is an env-var name, `target.url` is http(s) with a host, `judge.per_metric` keys are judged metrics, floats are finite, `n_resamples <= 10_000_000`; a declared family on an unrecognised id is trusted; `gemini/` ids map to family `gemini` (a judge only if the generator is not `gemini`); ids are NFKC-normalised; the loader refuses non-regular files and symlinks, nesting deeper than 32 and duplicate keys, and never echoes file content in errors | `docs/architecture/interfaces.md` §9, §15 |
| 2026-11-01 | T1.2 | (superseded by the row above for the default of `gated_slices`) `GatePolicy` checks an explicitly given `gated_slices` against `slices`; the shipped default `gated_slices` is exempt so `GatePolicy(metrics={}, slices=())` stays valid (prompt T1.2 test list). `judge.per_metric` keys must be in `KNOWN_METRICS`. `test_committed_configs.py` skips only files without a top-level `gate` key (rag-demo profiles); a gate config that does not load is a failure, not a skip | `core/config/gate.py`, `core/config/run.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.2 | **GII ids and long-§ rule:** `doc_id` is `gii-<law slug>-<number>` (`gii-bgb-622`, `gii-bgb-626a`, `gii-sgb-iv-7`; a § nested in an EGBGB Artikel `gii-egbgb-246a-1`), `chunk_id` is `<doc_id>#<i>`. `ParagraphChunker` emits one chunk per document; only when it has more than 2 000 tokens (`ingest.text.count_tokens`) **and** at least two Absätze, every Absatz becomes its own chunk (`section="Abs. N"`, same `citation_key`). Markers must grow (`1, 1a, 2`), so a list restarting at `(1)` is not an Absatz; a single Absatz is never cut, so 2 of 7 347 real chunks stay above the trigger (`gii-enwg-3#0`, 4 367 tokens, a definition list without Absätze; `gii-vag-7#0`, 2 409); 28 documents are split; an Absatz that is only `(N) (weggefallen)` is folded into its neighbour chunk (56 chunks under 8 tokens before) | `ingest/chunkers/paragraph.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.2 | **`version_date` of a GII document** is the `builddate` attribute of its `<norm>` (day GII built the consolidated text; `YYYYMMDDhhmmss` -> date), else the `builddate` of `<dokumente>`, else `None`. It is not the date of the last amendment: that free-text remark (`Zuletzt geändert durch Art. 6 G v. 23.7.2026 I Nr. 226`) is kept verbatim in `metadata["stand"]`. The pinned SHA-256 fixes the file, so the date is reproducible per pin; `--stats` prints it per law | `ingest/gii_xml.py`, `ingest/gii/norms.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.2 | **Untrusted XML and zip:** the zip is read in memory (never extracted; names with `..`, `/`, `\`, drive letters, symlinks, encrypted members, > 1 000 members, > 16 MiB per member, > 64 MiB in total or compression ratio > 200 are refused; reads are bounded, not trusted to the header). The XML is parsed with lxml `resolve_entities=False, no_network=True, load_dtd=False, huge_tree=False`, one shared options dict for the streaming parser and `hardened_parser()`; before or while parsing, any `<!ENTITY` or internal DTD subset, a non-UTF-8 encoding, > 16 MiB (the real maximum is 2.5 MB, ratio 6), nesting > 100, > 1 000 000 elements or > 100 000 norms is refused (the real files only *reference* `gii-norm.dtd`). libxml2 has no node limit without `huge_tree`, so the element cap bounds memory: worst case measured with a 14 MiB flat bomb is about 125 MiB extra RSS and 1.2 s (without the cap a 60 MiB file took 2 GB and 18 s). Override the limits with `ZipLimits` / `XmlLimits` for other sources. Every refusal is a counted problem in `loader.stats`, never an exception past the loader; `--stats` exits 1 when any exists | `ingest/gii/safezip.py`, `ingest/gii/xmlparse.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.2 | **New dependencies:** `lxml>=5.3` (runtime of `pruefstand`; `components.md` already lists it for `ingest`; streaming `iterparse` with explicit entity, DTD, network and depth control; binary wheels) and `types-lxml` (dev group, `mypy --strict`). `defusedxml` is not added: the stdlib/`defusedxml` ElementTree has no streaming release and no depth control, and the lxml parser plus the byte checks above cover XXE, entity bombs and DTD loading | `packages/pruefstand/pyproject.toml`, `pyproject.toml` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.2 | **Law identity and aliases:** the law of a norm is `<jurabk>` resolved through `law_aliases.yaml`, after stripping a trailing year (`BDSG 2018`, `VVG 2008`, `EnWG 2005`, ...). The 21 further national laws of `data/sources.yaml` are appended under `national:`; the GII spellings `BGBEG` (EGBGB), `SGB 4` (SGB IV), `KredWG` (KWG) and `TTDSG` (TDDDG) are aliases. Skip reasons counted per law: `header`, `toc`, `preamble`, `annex`, `range` (`(XXXX) §§ 3 bis 6`, `Art 27 bis 37`), `structure`, `repealed` (`(weggefallen)`, `(gegenstandslos)`), `placeholder` (text is `-` or only parenthesised lines such as `(Inkrafttreten)`; 15 norms), `empty`; `unknown_law`, `unknown_enbez`, `bad_citation_key`, `duplicate_doc_id`, `missing_law` are errors. `annex`: 31 text-bearing Anlagen (EGBGB Muster-Widerrufsbelehrung, BSIG Anlagen, ...) are skipped although they contain normative text, a conscious scope choice. `missing_law`: a law without archive is a counted problem (`--stats` exit 1) for the gii ids in `data/sources.yaml` (found at `<raw_dir>/../../sources.yaml`) or when fewer than `--expect-laws N` archives exist | `core/data/law_aliases.yaml`, `ingest/gii/norms.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.2 | **EGBGB articles:** `Art N` is a `gliederungseinheit` norm without `enbez` (text in the same norm) and `§ n` below it carries the Artikel in `gliederungsbez`. `core.citations` has no `Art. 246a § 1` form, so the nested unit gets the key of its Artikel (`Art. 246a EGBGB`) and the § goes to `metadata["section"]` / `Chunk.section` (`§ 1`, `§ 1 Abs. 2` when split); doc ids stay unique. A parser form with both levels would be a T1.5a change | `ingest/gii/norms.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.2 | **Two-letter § suffixes:** KWG § 53ca to § 53cq (17 norms) did not parse with the one-letter `core.citations` rule. The loader first counted them as skipped (`uncitable`, with a WARNING in `--stats`); the parser fix `fix/T1.5a-two-letter-section-suffix` (#17) is merged, the branch was removed, and the 17 norms are now ordinary documents. A norm whose number still does not parse is a counted `bad_citation_key` error | `ingest/gii_xml.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.2 | `Document.license` of GII documents is `§5-UrhG` (the prompt's test value), while `data/sources.yaml` calls the same licence `official-work-5-urhg`; T2.5 maps between them when it writes `CorpusManifest`. `Document.metadata` also carries `content_hash` (`stable_hash` of the NFC text, 16 hex) for change detection across pins | `ingest/gii_xml.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.2 | T2.1 follow-up: a list/dict `kind`, a non-mapping `sources` entry, a non-mapping `defaults` or `defaults.<kind>` and a YAML syntax error in `sources.yaml` raise `SourcesError` (exit 1) instead of a raw `TypeError`/`AttributeError`/`yaml.YAMLError` | `data/data_sources.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.2 | **`citation_key` is a label, not an id:** the units nested in one EGBGB Artikel share its key (Art. 229 EGBGB: 69 chunks), split chunks share their § key and § 1 KWG has 63 chunks with one key. T2.5 and the citation metrics must not assume uniqueness; `chunk_id` / `doc_id` are the ids. `ingest.text.join_hyphenation` is unused by T2.2 and kept for the line-wrapped texts of T2.3 and T2.4 | `ingest/gii_xml.py`, `ingest/text.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.3 | **Section map of court decisions:** a heading is a whole line (case-insensitive, optional `I.`/`A.`/`2.` enumerator and trailing colon, letter-spaced `T a t b e s t a n d :` read as one word, never tested beyond 48 characters). `Tenor`, `Tatbestand`, `Entscheidungsgründe` (aliases `Gründe`, `Aus den Gründen`) and `Leitsatz` (aliases `Leitsätze`, `Orientierungssatz`, the juris name of the same thing) are the only section names; `Verfahrensgang`, `Streitwertbeschluss` and `Rechtsmittelbelehrung` end the running section and start `section=None` text (as does text before the first heading); `Hinweis`, `Fußnoten`, `Anmerkung`, `Diese Entscheidung zitiert` and the other juris apparatus headings end the decision and everything after them is dropped; a bare `I.`/`II.` is not a boundary. Heading lines are not part of the chunk text. Measured on the 1k sample: 11 388 chunks (8 101 Entscheidungsgründe, 1 962 Tatbestand, 1 053 Tenor, 72 Leitsatz, 200 unnamed) | `ingest/chunkers/decision.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.3 | **Fallback and long-section chunking:** a decision without any of the four named sections (16 of 1 000, stat `no_sections`) is cut into `FixedTokenChunker` windows (512 approximate tokens, overlap 64, `section=None`). A named section of at most 512 tokens is one chunk; a longer one is split at Randnummern (a digit-only line that grows by 1 to 10; a list that restarts is not one) or, with fewer than two, at its lines, packed greedily to at most 512 tokens (no overlap between packed units), and one unit above 512 is cut into overlapping windows. Window arithmetic: starts at `0, M-O, 2(M-O), ...`, the last window ends at the last token (R9 D9-24). Tokens are the approximate `ingest.text.count_tokens` tokens; a `\w` run keeps its trailing combining marks so a boundary never splits a decomposed character | `ingest/chunkers/fixed.py`, `ingest/chunkers/decision.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.3 | **(Superseded by the review-fix row "New dependency `pyarrow`" below: the loader reads parquet with `pyarrow`, not polars.)** ~~**OLDP loader reads parquet with `polars`, no new dependency:** `pyarrow` and `datasets` are not dependencies of `pruefstand` (D19 accepts `polars` for analysis code; the call log already uses it), so `*.parquet` and `*.jsonl` below `data/raw/hf/oldp_decisions` are read with `polars` / the stdlib, only the needed columns, never the Hugging Face cache or the network. Untrusted-input limits (`OldpLimits`): `content` <= 2 M characters (the sample's maximum is 336 000), JSON line <= 16 MiB, data file <= 512 MiB, <= 10 000 decisions per load (the full dump is a later scale-up), <= 2 M graph rows; each breach is a counted problem, never an exception; HTML is parsed by lxml without network and `huge_tree`, the tree is walked iteratively. A record with empty `content` (2 of 1 000) is still a `Document` (empty text, no chunks, `stats.empty`), so the CLI line reads `decisions=1000`; `chunks`, `no_sections` ignore it~~ (the limits, the empty-content rule and the `max_documents` cap still hold) | `ingest/records.py`, `ingest/oldp.py`, `ingest/html_text.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.3 | **Court classification for T2.5:** `metadata["jurisdiction"]` comes from the court *name* (30 % of the sample have no `court.jurisdiction`): `Arbeitsgericht` (incl. Landes-/Bundesarbeitsgericht), `Sozialgericht`, `Zivilgericht` (Amts-/Land-/Oberlandesgericht, Kammergericht, BGH), `Verwaltungsgericht`, `Finanzgericht`, `Verfassungsgericht`, else `Sonstige`; the dump's own field is only the fallback. `metadata["tenant_domain"]` is `labour` for `Arbeitsgericht`, `insurance` when the first 4 000 characters of any other decision contain *versicherung* (case-insensitive; a documented keyword heuristic: 104 of 1 000), else `other`. `Document.license="ODbL-1.0"`, `source="oldp:decisions"`, `tenant="unassigned"`, `url=None` (no verified public URL scheme) | `ingest/oldp_courts.py`, `ingest/oldp.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.3 | **Citation graph keyed by decision id:** the graph rows name decisions only by `slug`, so `load_citation_graph(raw_dir, *, decisions_dir=None, limits=None)` resolves slugs against the sibling `oldp_decisions` directory (keyword-only additions to the `interfaces.md` §11 signature, call `load_citation_graph(raw_dir)` unchanged). Only `Case -> Law` edges count; an edge to a decision outside the decisions dump is `unresolved`. **Finding for T4.2:** the pinned 1k graph and 1k decisions samples share only 2 decisions (`oldp-161063` § 113 VwGO, `oldp-459718` § 519 ZPO; 856 of 858 law edges unresolved), so multi-hop questions need a graph/decisions pair pinned to the same selection. Law keys are `core.citations.normalise` output where the alias table knows the code (BGB, KSchG, GG, ...) and `"<section> <code>"` as-is otherwise (VwGO, ZPO, `AO 1977`), logged once per code (`citation_unknown_law`) | `ingest/citation_graph.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.3 | **Fixtures:** `tests/fixtures/oldp/*.json` are three real rows of the pinned sample (LAG Hessen 2 Sa 372/13, AG Düsseldorf 56 C 15412/01, OVG Sachsen 6 B 349/18), content trimmed at tag boundaries, `markdown_content`/`reference_markers` emptied; in the labour row every non-ASCII character is written as a named entity (`K&uuml;ndigung`) to exercise entity decoding. `tests/fixtures/oldp_graph/edges.jsonl` holds 5 edges in the dump's column names wired to those slugs. Helper modules beyond the prompt's file list, all inside `ingest/`: `html_text.py` (reused by T2.4), `records.py`, `oldp_courts.py` | `packages/pruefstand/tests/fixtures/oldp*` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.3 | **Review fixes (PR #19).** (a) `html_to_text` drops script/style/head/title with `etree.strip_elements(..., with_tail=False)` (C, linear); the per-element `drop_tree` loop was quadratic (160k adjacent `<script></script>x` took 35 s). (b) **New dependency `pyarrow`** (runtime of `pruefstand`; reason: polars cannot read parquet footers or stream a file, so row-group sizes could not be checked before decoding; mypy `ignore_missing_imports` for it because it ships no stubs, used only in `ingest/records.py`). Parquet is now read one row at a time (`iter_batches(batch_size=1)`) after checking each row group's *uncompressed* column sizes in the footer against `OldpLimits.max_group_bytes` (512 MiB); because a dictionary-encoded column can decode far larger than the footer says (a 3 KB file decoded to 240 MB), the decoded row is also capped by `max_record_bytes` (16 MiB, `record_too_large`). Measured: 12 rows of 20M characters peak at about 100 MB instead of 700 MB | `ingest/html_text.py`, `ingest/records.py`, `packages/pruefstand/pyproject.toml` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.3 | **Fail closed and visible trailer cuts.** `load_citation_graph` raises `OldpDataError` when the graph directory has no file, when no row is readable while problems were counted, or when `Case -> Law` edges exist but none resolve to a decision (an empty but intact graph returns `{}`); the optional `problems` list receives every counted problem. The apparatus-heading cut (`Fußnoten`, `Hinweis`, ...) applies only in the last quarter of a decision's lines (earlier occurrences are text), `dropped_trailer(text)` reports lines and characters, and the CLI line ends with `trailer_chars=N`. Fixed on the way: headings are lower-cased, not casefolded (`casefold` turned `Fußnoten` into `fussnoten`, so that heading never matched) | `ingest/citation_graph.py`, `ingest/chunkers/decision.py`, `ingest/oldp.py` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.3 | **No ADR for the `load_citation_graph` signature change:** the added `decisions_dir`, `limits` and `problems` parameters are keyword-only with defaults, additive, internal to `ingest`, and touch no ADR'd decision (ADR-0015 only constrains licence configs); the change is recorded in `interfaces.md` §11 and here. `OldpLimits` is defined in `interfaces.md` §11 | `docs/architecture/interfaces.md` |
| 2026-11-01 | T2.3 | **Hostile-input bounds, round 2.** (a) `records._parse_line` catches `ValueError` (superset of `JSONDecodeError`; Python refuses integers of more than 4 300 digits with a plain `ValueError`) and `RecursionError`, so a 10 KB line is one `bad_json`; ids must be positive 63-bit integers (`valid_id`). (b) `OldpLimits.max_rows` (now 100 000, the sample has about 1 000) caps the rows or lines *examined* per file and the rows per directory pass (loader, graph edges, slug scan), good or bad, with one counted `too_many_rows` (a truncated graph is therefore visible through `problems=`). (c) `ProblemLog` counts every problem exactly (`counts`, `total`) but keeps only the first 100 plus the first of each kind; `stats.problems.total` drives the CLI `errors=` and the exit code. A flood of 2 000 000 bad rows had cost 233 s and 449 MB. (d) `max_group_bytes` is 128 MiB (the real row group is 63 MiB). (e) Lone surrogates in any text field, including `court.*`, are `bad_record` (they cannot be encoded); a `date` that is not an ISO date is a counted `bad_date`, the decision is kept without date and the string never reaches the title. `pyarrow>=18` | `ingest/records.py`, `ingest/oldp.py`, `ingest/citation_graph.py` |

| Q1 | German annotator? | **Answered 2026-10-28: none.** Calibration from existing human data (D88) |
| Q2 | How is the A40 cluster accessed? | **Answered: plain SSH** (D90); round 10: Ollama-only on the cluster, reached from the TrueNAS VM through an `autossh` tunnel with an SSH key (D196, D202); since ADR-0018 one Ollama server on `:11434` for both GPUs (D324, D325); since ADR-0019 models on the cluster's own disk and one judge (D328–D332); key, alias and tunnel already set up (`docs/ops/setup-log.md`); the rest measured in T0.6 / T0.7 |
| Q3 | Paid APIs? | **Answered: free only** (D86) |
| Q4 | GitHub owner/repo and HF username? | Owner will provide; placeholders `dheena18/pruefstand`, `<HF_USER>/pruefstand-de` |
| Q5 | Demo hosting accounts? | **Answered round 11:** dashboard on GitHub Pages (D235); optional demo on the VM behind a free Cloudflare Tunnel (D239). Vercel, Fly.io and Hetzner are dropped |
| D453 | [T3.2] One app, two profiles: `RagConfig.profile` is `live` (tier 1, llama.cpp + TEI) or `gpu` (tier 2, Ollama native API). A `gpu` config is validated against the model catalogue `RAG_MODEL_CATALOGUE` (`infra/gpu/models.yaml`, copied into the image) at config load: `catalogue_id` must be `ollama/<tag>` (D221) and exist with the right role, `model` is the catalogue tag, `options` (`num_ctx`, `top_p`) equal the catalogue (Y3.2), `generation.temperature` and `generation.max_tokens` stay inside `option_bounds` (N2). `endpoint_env` URLs are read and checked at app start, never at config load, so `rag.gpu.yaml` loads in CPU CI |
| D454 | [T3.2] rag-demo never reaches a hosted provider (W3.15): endpoints (config `base_url` or the env variable named by `endpoint_env`) must be `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `llm-proxy` or an internal compose service name; an Ollama endpoint with a `/v1` path is rejected (D219). `ConfigError` is now also a `ValueError`, so pydantic validators can raise it |
| D455 | [T3.2] `/v1/info.models` is `{generator, generator_family, embed, rerank}` in both profiles (live: `embedding` renamed `embed`, `rerank` is `none` while the reranker is off); tier-2 `serving` = catalogue `engine`, `quantization`, `digest` (as `model_sha256`) plus `SERVING_ENGINE_VERSION` and `SERVING_GPU` from the environment. `embed.engine: ollama` starts with a `PendingOllamaEncoder` that fails every encode with `EmbeddingError` (503) until T3.4 adds `OllamaDenseEncoder`; `/v1/info.models.embedding` is renamed `embed` in `interfaces.md` §1.1 and the OpenAPI example (no schema change); the live endpoints (`LLM_URL`, `EMBED_URL`) are endpoint-checked too (W3.15); `LlamaCppGenerator` is renamed `OpenAICompatGenerator`, `OllamaChatGenerator` subclasses it (module `generate_ollama.py`) |
| D460 | [T3.3, compound splitter] An own dictionary splitter (`rag_demo/analysis/compound.py`) on a shipped lexicon (`lexicon_de.txt`, own list of ~330 German legal, HR and everyday word stems taken from the slice corpus, MIT with the repo, < 5 kB), not `charsplit`: the PyPI package `charsplit` 0.1.2 is an unrelated project that has no n-gram splitter, and the original CharSplit is not on PyPI. A word of 10 to 60 letters is split only if the lexicon covers it completely; linking elements `s es n en e er ens` between parts, the last part may carry an inflection ending (`n en e er ern s es ses`, so `Kündigungsfristen` gives `frist`); fewest parts, then fewest linking/ending characters; unknown words stay whole (false negatives preferred over false splits). Measured on the committed fixtures by a hand-labelled test (`test_compound.py`). Lexicon extension for the RQ1 compound axis is task **T3.3a** (`tasks/TASKS.md`) |
| D461 | [T3.3, hashing and index fingerprint] `SparseEncoder` token id = `xxhash64(token) mod 2**31` (dependency `xxhash`, BSD-2, replaces T1.13's blake2b mod 2**20); BM25 TF with k1 1.2, b 0.75; IDF from Qdrant `modifier: idf`. The `Indexer` computes `avgdl` (mean token count per chunk) from the corpus before encoding and stores it in the collection metadata with an `analyzer` fingerprint (hash scheme, dim, k1, b, `compound_split`, `stemmer`, `fold_umlauts`, digest of lexicon and stopword files). `create_app` compares the fingerprint with its own before it answers a query and answers 503 "…; re-index" on a missing or differing one (no silent dense-only fallback). Old indexes are invalid: default collection, snapshot and integration slice are renamed `slice_v2` |
| D462 | [T3.3, number normalisation] German thousands dots are removed and the decimal comma becomes `.` (`1.000,50` -> `1000.50`, `3,5` -> `3.5`; dates `01.02.2024` and lists `1,2,3` are left alone), `€` becomes the token `eur`; numbers are never stemmed |
| D463 | [T3.3, config, dependency] `RagConfig.analyzer` (`AnalyzerCfg`: `compound_split` true, `fold_umlauts` true, `stemmer` snowball_de) is part of `config_hash` (the run identity, so M4 = `compound_split: false` is a different candidate); it does not key a collection, the fingerprint of D461 does. The committed `rag*.yaml` and `bugs/*.yaml` rely on the defaults. With `stemmer: snowball_de` the `fold_umlauts` flag has no effect (Snowball German folds umlauts and `ue` itself); tokens longer than 64 characters are not stemmed. New dependencies `snowballstemmer` (BSD-3, pure Python, official Snowball German stemmer) and `xxhash`. Function abbreviations (`abs.`, `nr.`, `ggf.`, `i.v.m.` and others) are expanded only as whole words, law abbreviations stay; stopwords are the German Snowball list in `analysis/stopwords_de.txt` |
| D490 | [T3.4, collection naming and alias] The default collection is the index fingerprint `chunks__<corpus_version>__<analyzer_hash8>__<embed_slug>` (`index/naming.py`; slugs keep `[a-z0-9-]`, `analyzer_hash8` = first 8 hex of sha256 over the T3.3 analyzer fingerprint, so a changed `compound_split`, stemmer, lexicon or hash scheme gives a new collection); the alias `chunks_active` is switched in **one** atomic `POST /collections/aliases` (delete + create) only after every point is written and the metadata is final. `--collection <name>` overrides the name (PR jobs: `pr_<N>_<shortsha>` with `--no-alias`, which never touches any alias); `--drop <name>` deletes a collection and refuses the one `chunks_active` points to. The app still serves `RAG_COLLECTION` (default `slice_v2`, D461); pointing it at `chunks_active` is a deployment setting, the Qdrant path accepts alias names |
| D491 | [T3.4, idempotence and metadata] A re-run on an existing collection upserts the same UUID5 point ids (`uuid5(NAMESPACE, chunk_id)`, namespace unchanged from T1.13), deletes points whose id is no longer in the corpus (`must_not has_id` filter) and rewrites the collection metadata (`PATCH /collections/<c>`): `corpus_version`, `tenants`, the analyzer fingerprint and `avgdl` (D461) plus `embed_model`, `dim` and the index-time `truncated_chunks`. An existing collection with another dense size is refused (`IndexMismatchError`, drop it first). Payload = `chunk_id, doc_id, tenant_id, citation_key, section, lang, text, source, is_attack, version_date`; payload indexes `tenant_id` (keyword, `is_tenant=true`), `lang`, `doc_id` (keyword), `is_attack` (bool) |
| D492 | [T3.4, encoders] `rag_demo/dense.py` is deleted; `TeiDenseEncoder` (tier 1) and the new `OllamaDenseEncoder` (tier 2) live in `rag_demo/index/encoders.py`: batch 32 (was 16), timeout, 3 attempts with 0.5 s / 1 s backoff on connection errors, timeouts and HTTP 408/425/429/5xx, no retry on other 4xx, then `EncoderError` (subclass of `EmbeddingError`, so the API still maps it to 503). Ollama: native `POST <url>/api/embed` only, body `{model: <bare tag>, input, truncate: true, options}`, never `keep_alive`; `options` = the catalogue entry's `options` exactly (`wiring.catalogue_options`, integer keys sent as integers) so Ollama does not reload the model, and `options.num_ctx` must equal `embed.max_input_tokens` (`ConfigError` otherwise). Vectors are checked (count, dimension, non-zero) and normalised to unit length. The bearer header (D216) is read once at construction and never appears in logs or errors; Qdrant errors strip `QDRANT_API_KEY` from the shown body. `PendingOllamaEncoder` (D455) is removed: tier-2 queries now embed with `OllamaDenseEncoder` |
| D493 | [T3.4, dependencies, tokenizer] New rag-demo dependencies: `typer` (the `rag-demo` console script, MIT, already in the lock), `jsonschema` (moved from the optional `index` extra: the CLI validates every corpus line, MIT, already in the lock) and `tokenizers>=0.21` (Hugging Face, Apache-2.0, already in the lock via litellm; D241). `truncated_chunks` counts passages above `max_input_tokens` with the model's `tokenizer.json` (`index/tokens.py`; without a file a word-and-punctuation lower bound). The catalogue entry `ollama/bge-m3` now carries `tokenizer: {source: hf:BAAI/bge-m3/tokenizer.json, sha256: 21106b6d...dd08}` (the existing optional catalogue field, AI13-05); `python -m rag_demo.index.fetch_tokenizer` downloads it and verifies the digest; the Dockerfile bakes it into `/app/tokenizers/bge-m3.json` (`RAG_EMBED_TOKENIZER`), `make fetch-tokenizer` fetches it for the VM. `rag-demo index --encoder ollama` refuses to run without `RAG_EMBED_TOKENIZER` so the stored count is real. The 17 MB file itself is never committed; unit tests build a real word-level `tokenizers` tokenizer, one `network` test downloads the pinned file |
| D494 | [T3.4, corpus reader, layout] The corpus reader streams `<tenant>.chunks.jsonl` line by line, validates each line against the shipped schema copy `rag_demo/index/chunk.schema.json` (byte-equal to `schemas/chunk.schema.json`, tested; it sits beside the code because `rag_demo/schemas.py` already exists, so a `rag_demo/schemas/` directory would shadow nothing but confuse imports) and fails with `<file>:<line>: ...`, also for invalid JSON and for a row whose `tenant` is not the file's tenant. `rag_demo/index.py` became the package `rag_demo/index/` (`corpus_reader`, `encoders`, `indexer`, `naming`, `qdrant_admin`, `tokens`, `fetch_tokenizer`); the package re-exports nothing. The tiny test corpus `apps/rag-demo/tests/fixtures/corpus/` (40 real chunks of `corpus/slice_v1`, 4 tenants, own manifest) lets app tests run without reading `corpus/` of the repo root. `make index-slice` runs `rag-demo index --collection slice_v2` and then only snapshots; a snapshot restore re-creates `chunks_active` (`tests/integration/conftest.py`) |
| D480 | [T4.1, assignment order and drops] `assign_splits` draws whole clusters in a fixed order so no split is starved: `robust_pr`, `rq1`, `smoke`, `core`, `full` (refusal stratum, then three blocks), then the judged subset of `core`; every random choice is one seeded permutation of the id-sorted candidates. Whatever no stage takes is *dropped and counted*: `assign_splits_report` returns `SplitReport(cases, dropped, n_perturbed)` (`assign_splits` returns `cases` only); `assignment_summary(cases, seed)` gives the manifest fields `blocks` / `judged_subset` / `assignment_seed`. A cluster whose unperturbed cases have more than one role (answerable / unanswerable / injection / probe) is not placed (builders keep roles apart), and a cluster that lands in `smoke` or `core` keeps its unperturbed cases only (variants dropped), so `core` is exactly 220 + 50 + 30 and perturbed cases stay in `perturbed_all` (X3.6). | T4.1 |
| D481 | [T4.1, exact quotas] A quota is met exactly with whole clusters; when nothing fits the remainder any more, the first skipped cluster is trimmed to the missing unperturbed cases (its other cases are dropped and counted), so a quota fails only when too few cases exist (`DatasetError` naming split, group and counts). `robust_pr` and `rq1`: one base per cluster (the one with the most applicable perturbations, ties by id), bases ranked by applicability score then id; `robust_pr` also needs `robust_pr_min_bases_per_type` bases for each of the 11 names in `SplitPlan.robust_pr_types` and `robust_pr_min_perturbed` perturbed cases. `full`: refusal stratum and blocks stratified by tenant x category (largest remainder), perturbed variants dealt first (balanced per block, at most `full_block_perturbed` each, base in the same block), plain clusters then by (stratum count, block size). The judged subset is stratified by group (answerable / unanswerable / injection, shares as in `core`: 110 / 25 / 15) and by tenant inside each group (P3.4, no `lang`). | T4.1 |
| D482 | [T4.1, `SplitPlan` and `DatasetBuildConfig` fields] `SplitPlan` (frozen, extra forbid, sizes >= 0) holds every number of the plan table: `smoke_size` 50 / `smoke_min_per_category` 3, `core_answerable` 220 / `core_unanswerable` 50 / `core_injection` 30, `core_min_cross_lingual` 30, `core_min_answerable_clusters` 200, `min_clusters_per_tenant` 30, `judged_subset_size` 150, `robust_pr_bases` 40 / `robust_pr_min_bases_per_type` 20 / `robust_pr_min_perturbed` 300 / `robust_pr_types` (the 11 names of SPEC 4.1), `full_refusal` 150 within `full_refusal_min` 135 .. `full_refusal_max` 165 on `full_refusal_min_clusters` 100, `full_block_unperturbed` 300 / `full_block_perturbed` 150 / `full_block_min_clusters` 250, `nightly_cap` 600, `rq1_bases` 300; a size of 0 switches that part off (tests). `DatasetBuildConfig`: `version` 1, `name`, `dataset_version`, `corpus_dir`, `out_dir` (`datasets`), `split_plan`, `seed`, `prompt_versions`. Pre-approved by the T4.1 prompt. | T4.1 |
| D483 | [T4.1, `validate_dataset`] Always problems: schema errors (`<split>.jsonl:<line>`, never the row), duplicate ids, `base_id` of a missing case, `license_config` outside `laws_eurlex`, `germanquad_derived`, `germanrag_derived`, `oldp_derived`, `own` (the set of DATA_LICENSES section 2) or missing from the manifest, a cluster mixing two of the share-alike configs (`oldp_derived`, `germanquad_derived`, `germanrag_derived`), a cluster over two splits (named in split order, `spans smoke,core`; `smoke_live` and `human_labels` reuse cases and are exempt), a `full` case with a wrong `block`, a cluster over two blocks, `corpus_version` and counts differing from the manifest, a `robust_pr` case that is neither a base nor a variant. Quota findings use `SplitPlan()` defaults and are problems on real builds, but logged warnings (structlog `dataset_quota_not_met`, not returned) under `datasets/fixtures/*`; a missing `smoke`, `core`, `robust_pr` or `full` split counts as a quota finding, `rq1` is checked when present. | T4.1 |
| D484 | [T4.1, HF export layout] `export_hf` writes `<license_config>/<split>.jsonl` (one folder per config, only splits with rows) and `README.md` rendered from `dataset/card_template.md.j2`; the YAML header lists `configs:` (config_name + data_files, no extra keys, so `load_dataset(repo, config)` keeps working) and a top-level `config_licenses:` mapping config -> SPDX id, with `license: other`. Attribution texts are in `dataset/licenses.py` (same wording as DATA_LICENSES section 3). Refused: a directory under `datasets/private/`, an unknown licence config; filtered: cases tagged `heldout`, `held_out` or `private`. `--dry-run --out DIR` builds the layout directly in `DIR` (never inside the dataset directory); `export_hf(..., dry_run=True)` uses `dir/hf_export` (git-ignored). The upload needs `HF_TOKEN` from the environment, creates the repo as private unless `--public`, and builds the layout in a temp dir. | T4.1 |
| D485 | [T4.1, dependencies] `huggingface-hub>=2.4.0` becomes a runtime dependency of `pruefstand` (the real upload; it was a dev dependency); `datasets>=5.1.0` joins the dev group (new transitive dev-only packages `dill`, `multiprocess`, `pandas`, all permissive licences; `fsspec` moves 2026.9.0 -> 2026.7.0 in `uv.lock`) (the load check of the export layout and its test; `export_hf` skips the check with a warning when `datasets` is not importable, e.g. because the repo's own `datasets/` folder shadows it as a namespace package). | T4.1 |
| D486 | [T4.1, review fixes] `SplitReport.reasons` counts dropped cases by `surplus`, `mixed_role`, `trimmed`, `variants_dropped` and `assign_splits_report` logs `splits_dropped`; `full` blocks are dealt per tenant x category stratum with remainders going to the smallest blocks (spread <= 1 cluster also with variants); `validate_dataset` checks a variant shares its base's `cluster_id` (`TestCase` has no `parallel_id` field, so DE/EN parallel questions are covered by the shared-DE-doc cluster rule only); `export_hf` reads the token via `PruefstandSettings.hf_token` (`HF_TOKEN` or `PRUEFSTAND_HF_TOKEN`) and replaces an existing output directory only when it holds its marker file `.pruefstand-hf-export`; a smoke minimum larger than `smoke_size` is trimmed. | T4.1 |
| D495 | [T3.4 review, supersedes parts of D461, D490, D491, D493] (a) **Fresh collection per build:** a derived name gets a build suffix (`<fingerprint>__b<8 hex>`), so the app never reads a collection that is being written; the alias `chunks_active` moves in one atomic request after the metadata says `complete`; the previous alias target is deleted only after the swap; a build that raises drops its partial collection and leaves the alias untouched. An explicit `--collection` (PR jobs) is dropped and rebuilt if it exists, and refused when it is the served collection. In-place upsert and the stale-point prune of D491 are removed. (b) **`complete`:** the collection is created with `metadata.complete=false`; only the final metadata update after the last point sets `true`; the app answers 503 "index build is not complete; re-index" for a collection without `complete: true` (so an aborted `--no-alias` build is never served). (c) **Served default is the alias:** `RAG_COLLECTION` defaults to `chunks_active` (was `slice_v2`, D461) for tier 1 and tier 2; `make index-slice` builds through `rag-demo index` and snapshots the alias target; the integration restore recreates the alias. (d) The app re-reads the collection metadata every 10 s, so an alias swap is picked up without a restart. (e) The bge-m3 tokenizer source is pinned to the Hugging Face commit `5617a9f61b028005a4858fdac845db406aefb181` (`hf:BAAI/bge-m3@<rev>/tokenizer.json`), the sha256 check stays. Known limits (follow-up): `read_corpus` loads all rows into memory and the embedding pass is single-threaded, fine for the slice, to be streamed for the full corpus |
| D500 | [T1.12, Action image] `action/Dockerfile` follows the T0.3 template and needs the **repository root** as build context (it installs `pruefstand` from the workspace); GitHub builds a `uses: ./action` step with `action/` as context, so that form works only after T9.1b publishes the image and `action.yml` points at it. Until then `action-selftest.yml` builds the image from the root and runs it with `docker run --network host --user "$(id -u):$(id -g)"` (uid 10001 cannot write the runner-owned workspace; CLAUDE.md section 8 keeps the non-root user). `action/entrypoint.py` reads `INPUT_*`, calls the CLI through an injectable `invoke` (subprocess with argument lists in production; the unit tests run the real CLI in-process under respx with the T1.10 recordings), exits with the gate exit code and rejects an empty `baseline-run`, a missing file or an unknown `tier` with exit 2. The unit test asserts the title `## Prüfstand tier 1: ✅ PASS` (the renderer titles every `stack=live` run so), not the prompt's older `## Prüfstand: ✅ PASS` |
| D501 | [T1.12, live-e2e plan and cost] Job `prepare` decides which jobs run: PRs that change only docs (`docs/`, `prompts/`, `tasks/`, `*.md`, `mkdocs.yml`) skip `live` and `scenarios` with success (ci-cd.md section 1, "docs-only PRs exit early"); the scenario matrix runs on `schedule`, `workflow_dispatch` and PRs that touch `apps/rag-demo/**`, `.github/workflows/live-e2e.yml` or `.github/actions/live-stack/**` (so a change to the workflow exercises it). `workflow_dispatch` gets the input `job` (`all` or `scenarios`; `dose-response` and `judge-anchor` are added by their tasks) next to `probe_quota`, which runs only `quota-probe`. The aggregate `gate` accepts a skipped job only when the plan says so. `prepare` holds `actions: read` for `gh cache list` (allow-list entry in `scripts/workflow_policy.py`) |
| D502 | [T1.12, first run without a main baseline] The prompt says to run the baseline "from the base SHA's rag-demo config"; that needs a second app instance with a mounted config. Instead, with no successful `push` run on `main` (or an expired artefact), `live` gates the candidate against a **second run of the same commit** and puts "No tier-1 baseline on main yet" under the marker line. Hard fails and the `error_rate` check come from the candidate alone, so the gate exit code is kept (a first-run tenant leak still fails; the prompt's "exit 0" would hide it); the paired rows carry no signal at n = 20 anyway (X5.6). The judge line "Judge (informational): ok / skipped (fork PR \| quota) - N calls" is added to `comment.md` by the workflow from `run.json` (`judge_mode`, `cost.llm_calls`); the renderer is unchanged |
| D503 | [T1.12, caches and stack] The shared steps live in the composite action `.github/actions/live-stack` (restore models and slice snapshot, fetch on miss, warm the rag-demo layer cache with `docker/build-push-action`, `make up PROFILES="core live" WAIT=1`, restore the snapshot with `scripts/slice/restore_snapshot.py` or `make index-slice`). Cache **saves** are workflow steps of `prepare`, guarded by `github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'` and a cache miss, and run after the stack was up once, so the models cache also holds the TEI weights downloaded into `~/.cache/pruefstand/models/tei`; PR jobs only restore. The model key is the one of `ci.yml` (`models-<hash infra/models/*.yaml>`), so both share the cache |
| D504 | [T1.12, comment job] The `comment` job checks `comment.md` with `action/check_comment.py` from the base SHA; in the one PR that adds that file the base has none, so the job falls back to the same two checks in shell (marker first line, at most 60 000 characters). `action-selftest.yml` posts **no** PR comment (the ci-cd.md row said it would): its reports go to the job summary and the `selftest-report` artefact, which keeps `pull-requests: write` out of a job that runs the Action image |
| D505 | [T1.12, T1.13 quality probe on the hosted runner] First PR run: the `live`-marked `test_smoke_questions_cite_the_expected_norm_within_the_latency_budget` of T1.13 fails on the hosted runner (cited 5/10 < 6, p95 21 s > 15 s with the 0.5B emergency generator, D375), while every deterministic check, the five scenarios and the selftest pass. The probe runs as its own step with `continue-on-error: true` (reported in the log, not gating) and is deselected in the gating `live` test step; the marker expression is unchanged. Tracked as **T1.13b** (tasks/TASKS.md): recalibrate the threshold or the generator for the hosted runner, then move the probe back into the gating step. Until then the probe's verdict and measurement are written to the job summary and to `comment.md` as an explicit "reported, not gating" line The comment's judge line says "skipped (no judged metrics yet, T5.2b)" until judged metrics exist |
| D510 | [T3.5, retrieval package] `rag_demo/retrieve.py` is deleted; `rag_demo/retrieval/` holds `filters` (`allowed_tenants`, `tenant_filter`), `query_builder` (`build_query`, pure, returns a frozen `QdrantQuery`), `bugs` (the seeded M8/M9 code paths, unchanged behaviour), `store` (`QdrantStore`), `corpus_meta` (`CorpusMeta`), `rerank`, `retriever` (`QdrantRetriever`) and `testing` (`assert_tenant_scoped`, reused by T3.7 and T7.1a). The tenant filter is built once, in `filters.tenant_filter`; `build_query` is the only builder the retriever calls for the main query. With `tenant_filter: inside` every prefetch **and** the top-level query carry the filter (defence in depth); `assert_tenant_scoped` accepts the object or the recorded wire body and fails on a missing, partial, `should`, shared-public-only or extra unfiltered leg. `allowed_tenants` raises `ValueError` for a malformed tenant (lower-case slug, <= 64) and, when given `known`, for any tenant outside it; the API keeps checking membership against the collection metadata (422) |
| D511 | [T3.5, query shape] Prefetch limits `retrieval.prefetch_k` 50 per leg, fusion `{"rrf": {"k": 60}}` sent explicitly (`retrieval.rrf_k`; Qdrant 1.16+ uses k = 2 when none is given), `retrieval.hybrid: false` = one filtered dense prefetch (fused with RRF too, so scores stay comparable). The fused query asks for `max(top_k, rerank.top_n)` hits when `reranker: on`, else `top_k`. Both fields are new `RagConfig` sections (`RetrievalCfg`), part of `config_hash` with their defaults, so every config hash changes once with this task (like D463) |
| D512 | [T3.5, post-condition guard] After rerank and cut, a hit whose tenant is not in `allowed_tenants` raises `TenantIsolationError` (a `RetrievalError`, so the API answers 503 `backend unavailable`) and logs the chunk ids at ERROR, never the text. The guard is skipped while `tenant_filter: post_topk` or `shared_public_merge: unfiltered` is active (those switches must produce real leaks) and can be switched off with `guard=False` for the isolation tests, which prove the filter alone. The leak bug paths run before the reranker; with `post_topk` the first stage is cut at `top_k` as before |
| D513 | [T3.5, reranker] `CrossEncoderReranker` posts **one** batched `POST <RERANK_URL>/rerank` `{query, texts, truncate: true}` with the first `rerank.top_n` (10) passages, each cut client-side to the catalogue entry's `max_input_tokens` (256) by a word-and-punctuation count (a lower bound of the model's tokens, so it never cuts text the model would keep; the service enforces the exact limit), sends no `Authorization` header and reads no key (D217), and uses the catalogue's new optional `request_timeout_s` (60 for `cpu/bge-reranker-v2-m3`; `infra/gpu/catalogue_schema.py` gains the field). The reranked count is `max(rerank.top_n, top_k)` (a request with `top_k` above `top_n` reranks all returned hits, so a result never mixes cross-encoder and RRF scores). The result is the scored hits sorted by score (ties keep the fused order) followed by the unscored remainder, ranks 1..n, then cut to `top_k`; all scores are reranker scores. A failing or malformed answer raises `RerankError` (503): the retrieval never degrades silently to the fused order. There is no retry (an infra failure is retried by the executor, D226). `reranker: off` builds `IdentityReranker`; `reranker: on` needs `RERANK_URL` (checked at app start like the other endpoints) |
| D514 | [T3.5, config] `RerankCfg.enabled` (T3.2 placeholder) is removed: `RagConfig.reranker` is the only switch, `rerank` holds `endpoint_env`/`base_url`, `top_n` (10) and `on_retrieve` (true, DEV12-09/D271). The T3.2 validator that rejected `reranker: on` is removed. `rag.gpu.yaml` and every `configs/bugs/gpu/*.yaml` set `reranker: "on"`; new `configs/bugs/gpu/reranker_off.yaml` is M1 (one switch); tier 1 stays `off`. `/v1/info.models.rerank` is the catalogue id when the reranker is on (`rerank-cpu` for a live-profile config that turns it on), else `none`. `Hit` gains optional `section` and `lang` (from the payload) |

The 10 s metadata cache now takes an injectable clock |

With `rerank.on_retrieve: false` the app serves it from the same retriever with `IdentityReranker` (`with_reranker`), so `/v1/query` is unaffected and the interface `retrieve(tenant_id, query, top_k)` is unchanged. The OpenAPI file already listed the endpoint (T1.1/T1.3); the rag-demo contract test no longer excludes it. `retrieve_ok.json` is recorded from the real app by `scripts/models/record_contract.py` (`make cassettes-contract`, convention `source: recorded` with the model pins, not the prompt's `rag-demo-recorded`, because `test_target_api_v1.py` accepts only `openapi-example` and `recorded`); until task **T3.5a** re-records it the file keeps its `openapi-example` origin |

The T1.13 integration conftest builds the retriever through `rd_support.build_test_retriever`. Accepted: the 10 s metadata cache can serve an index state up to 10 s old after an alias swap (D495). Review minors: a malformed tenant listed in the index raises `UnknownTenantError` (API 422); a `gpu` config must set `reranker` explicitly (the default `off` is the M1 bug value) |

| D521 | [T5.1, Ollama path] `build_backend("ollama/<tag>")` reads only `endpoints.yaml` (`base_url_env`, loopback/private URL enforced; an id the table does not list uses `OLLAMA_GPU0_URL`); the catalogue is read when a request is made (missing id -> `ConfigError` naming it). Every request carries the five catalogue options (caller values replaced, DEBUG log), `think: false`, no `keep_alive`, on `/api/chat` through a size-capped transport (`LimitedTransport` in a LiteLLM `AsyncHTTPHandler`); pure request building moved to `backends/kwargs.py` | D219-D221, D252 | [0009](adr/0009-litellm-providers.md) |
| D522 | [T5.1, retries and quota] `RetryPolicy` defaults: 3 retries, 1 s base doubling to 30 s, 25 % seeded jitter, `Retry-After` honoured, `Retry-After` > 60 s = daily limit. External-provider 429 after the retries -> `QuotaExhausted`, model exhausted for the run (no network call afterwards, cache hits still served); local-provider 429 stays a transient `LLMError`. A configured fallback backend is policy-checked and marked `fallback=True`. `RateLimitedError(LLMError)` carries `retry_after_s` (headers are read from the exception chain, LiteLLM drops them on its own wrapper) | W1.4, D97, Y4.4 | [0009](adr/0009-litellm-providers.md) |
| D523 | [T5.1, cache key] Key = interfaces §8 with `model_version` = provider version + `#e<epoch>`. Version probe once per model and run: Ollama `tag@digest[:12]` from `/api/tags` (warning when it differs from the catalogue pin), llama.cpp hash of `/props` `model_path`, GitHub Models from the first response (so the first GitHub call per model and run is never a hit); a failed probe leaves the version empty, the call that follows reports a real outage. Interface change (flagged): `LLMClient` gains keyword-only `retry` and `epochs`; `cache_epochs.py` (`current_epoch`, `invalidate_judge`, `clear_drift`, `read_drift`) works on `cache_epochs.json` / `judge_drift.json` in `PRUEFSTAND_CACHE_DIR`; epoch != `judge_epoch`, never in `eval_fingerprint`. Review fixes: for `ollama/` the version string also carries a hash of the effective catalogue options and `think=false` (a changed `num_predict` misses without a new digest); a probeable provider (Ollama, llama.cpp) whose version is unknown neither reads nor writes the cache; `complete_json` caches only validated answers, empty answers are never cached; `build_client` requires a `canaries` argument and refuses None (fail closed); a `Retry-After` above the cap ends retries for local providers too; an exhausted fallback is marked exhausted | W3.2, W3.3, D113, D114, X3.3 | [0009](adr/0009-litellm-providers.md) |

| D560 | [T3.1e, wrapper op `log [N]`, ADR-0019 addendum] The wrapper `pruefstand-cluster` gets the third read-only op (after `status` and `show`; the seventh op overall) `log [N]` (`N` an integer 1..1000, default 200, anything else exit 64; at most one argument). Like `status` and `show` it needs no lock, no `MAINTENANCE` and no allow-list entry, and it is the only change next to sudo: the sudoers file and the sudo rule are byte-identical (one wrapper path, `env_keep` of `PRUEFSTAND_LOCK_HELD`). It runs `bash <bin>/log-tail.sh <N>` on the cluster; `cluster_ssh.sh` `cluster_op log [N]` mirrors it with the same checks |
| D561 | [T3.1e, `infra/cluster/log-tail.sh`] Pipeline with bounded memory: `tail -n N` of `OLLAMA_LOG` (cluster.env), `cut -b1-4000` per line, control characters dropped, sed redaction, `cut -b1-2000` per line (total output at most 2 MB). Redaction on the cluster: `%2F`-style escapes decoded first; `OLLAMA_MODELS` -> `<models-dir>`, the `ollama serve` binary -> `<ollama-bin>`, `$HOME` and `/home/<x>` -> `<home>`, the login -> `<cluster-user>` and the host name -> `<host>` (a name of 4+ characters anywhere, also inside `user_x`, `x2`, `x-y`; a shorter one between non-alphanumerics), `/data/<x>` -> `<data-dir>`, every IPv4/IPv6 address, URL host, `localhost`, `user@host`, dotted `host:port`, `port N`/`"port":N`/`-p N` except `127.0.0.1:11434`, GPU uuids, sha256 digests, secrets (Bearer/Basic values, key=value pairs whose key names a secret/password/credential/api key, a `token`/`key` pair with a 12+ character value, well-known token prefixes, JWTs, PEM and ssh keys, runs of 32+ token characters not starting with `/`). Kept on purpose: `file.go:480` pairs, `std::`, `BOS token = 11`, `n_tokens = 512`, absolute library paths and versions, and every load number (slots, KV size, layers offloaded, ctx size, `Parallel:`/`KvSize:`) |
| D562 | [T3.1e, fail closed, what the scan covers] The redacted text is held back and scanned once more; the scan is a safety net, not a second redaction. It looks for the login, the host name, `$HOME`, the models dir, `/data/` and `/home/` paths, any IPv4 other than `127.0.0.1:11434`, IPv6 with `::`, URL hosts, `user@host`, GPU uuids, sha256 digests, 32+ character alphanumeric runs and secret-looking pairs. It does NOT cover bare hostnames, dotted `host:port` or plain `port N` values and path-like long identifiers: those depend on the sed rules alone. A failed tail, a failed sed or a scan hit prints nothing on stdout and exits non-zero (1 log or cluster.env unusable, 5 redaction failed or leak, 64 usage); the message names no log content. `log-tail.sh` is in `SHA256SUMS` like every `infra/cluster/*.sh` (`bin-integrity`, `bin-sums`, health.sh exit 14), so the owner redeploys with `make gpu-deploy-bin` after the merge |
| D600 | [T3.1b, candidate pass] `make bench-models CANDIDATE=<catalogue tag>` runs `model_selection.bench_candidate` under `pruefstand-lock` for ONE pulled catalogue model of role generator or judge: first-load time (cold when not resident), German smoke with the seeded bootstrap CI, judge-JSON validity, throughput at concurrency 1 and 4, VRAM from the wrapper `status` and `/api/ps`; rows are appended to `results/<date>.csv` (phases `cand_*`, `run_id` `cand-...`), answers go to `<date>_<tag>_answers.jsonl`. It refuses an unpulled tag and never pulls, removes or swaps (ADR-0019 swap rule stays with the lead); embeddings and rerankers stay with `embed_bench.py`. `OllamaClient` gains the read-only native `/api/tags`. |

It still contains the KV cache at the pinned `num_ctx` and slots, so it overestimates the vision tower plus compute buffer; it is NOT yet a value for `fits()` or `models.yaml`. The load log (follow-up: wrapper op) replaces it. |

Planned from 19 laws, seed 20261103, round-robin, norms of 300-1800 characters, T1.13 slice norms excluded, 40 reserve passages. `indomain_dev_sources.json` (`source_passage_ids` = all 240 planned, `used_passage_ids` = final) is the exclusion list T4.x must load and drop from every split. No private doc, canary or held-out text is used or sent. Law choice: 19 of the 30 pinned GII laws, the employment, data-protection, contract/commercial, copyright, product-liability and insurance laws of the product scope (the demo tenants); EGBGB, the energy and BSI laws, ArbGG, SGB IV, AktG, InsO, KWG and VAG are left out because 19 laws already give 4 355 norms for 240 passages (a choice, not a defect). UWG is left out for a defect: its pinned `xml.zip` SHA-256 in `data/sources.yaml` is stale (the upstream archive changed, download checksum mismatch 2026-11-03), so the T2.1b refresh row in `tasks/TASKS.md` must re-pin it (`corpus.yaml:77` still needs `gii:uwg`). The file path is under `experiments/` and no allowlist change is needed (`check_no_raw_data.py` bans archives and parquet, not jsonl under 1 MB). |

Provisional models: generator `openai/gpt-4.1-mini`, checker `meta/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct` (T4.2 pins the real datagen model; ids unverified until the first successful call). Credential `PRUEFSTAND_GH_MODELS_TOKEN` only, never printed; all answers cached on disk, a 429 stops the run with exit code 3. |

Generation of `indomain_dev.jsonl` therefore runs on the owner's laptop or in an Actions job with `models: read`; until then the card and the sources file carry status `planned`. Open: is it the VM egress or the endpoint? |
